0	# Todo
1	
2	This file uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
3	contains near term tasks with a short description and
4	uses links or reference links for more details.
5	
6	## In Progress
7	
8	**refactor: versioned primitive module dirs**
9	
10	The three primitives sit at uneven depths — `mpsc/` is a
11	directory, SPSC is spread across `lib.rs` plus top-level
12	`producer.rs` / `consumer.rs`, and the pool is one
13	`pool.rs` — and there is nowhere for a second implementation
14	of a primitive to live for A/B comparison in iiac-perf. Give
15	each primitive a `{primitive}/v0/` module dir so all three
16	are siblings and a future `v1` slots in beside `v0` as a
17	peer.
18	
19	Decisions (the versioning discussion, folded in):
20	
21	- **Structure** — `src/spsc/v0/`, `src/mpsc/v0/`,
22	  `src/pool/v0/`; version is the inner axis, so each
23	  primitive versions independently.
24	- **Names unchanged** — keep the current type names inside
25	  `v0` (`Ring`, `MpscRing`, `Pool`, …); a champion alias per
26	  module (`spsc::Ring = spsc::v0::Ring`) plus the existing
27	  crate-root re-exports keep the public API identical, so the
28	  cycle is a pure move. `spsc::v0::Ring` is newly reachable
29	  for explicit version pinning.
30	- **Shared core stays in `lib.rs`** — `Error`,
31	  `CACHE_LINE_SIZE`, `USER_WORDS`, `slot_ptr`, `check_type`,
32	  `CacheAligned` are crate-wide; versions reach them via
33	  `crate::`.
34	- **`Full` / `Empty` promote to the shared core** — defined
35	  in the SPSC endpoint files today but imported by mpsc
36	  (`crate::{Empty, Full}`); leaving them inside `spsc/v0/`
37	  would couple sibling primitives (and any future
38	  `spsc::v1`) to one version's module. Crate-root re-exports
39	  keep the public API unchanged.
40	- **`-1` also moves the SPSC core out of `lib.rs`** —
41	  `Header`, `Ring`, `MAGIC`, `LAYOUT_VERSION`, `header_ptr`,
42	  `region_size`, `validate_geometry` go into `spsc/v0/`
43	  alongside `producer.rs` / `consumer.rs`.
44	- **registry stays top-level** — its placement waits for the
45	  descriptor-queue endpoints work.
46	- **Cargo feature-gating deferred** — coexistence needs no
47	  features; per-version build-size exclusion only pays off
48	  once a `v1` exists, so features land with the first fork.
49	- **Close-out doc sync** — `ring-buffer-design.md`'s
50	  as-built intro names top-level `producer.rs` /
51	  `consumer.rs`; sync it to the new layout at close-out.
52	
53	Plan ladder:
54	
55	- `0.12.0-0` chore: open versioned module dir refactor
56	  (done)
57	- `0.12.0-1` refactor: spsc into a v0 module dir (done)
58	- `0.12.0-2` refactor: mpsc into a v0 module dir
59	- `0.12.0-3` refactor: pool into a v0 module dir

1	//! MPSC ring: multi-producer single-consumer sibling of the
2	//! SPSC ring, per the design doc's "MPSC ring (sibling
3	//! primitive)" section:
4	//!
5	//! - Same region shape as the SPSC ring — a four-line
6	//!   [`MpscHeader`] then slots — plus a per-slot sequence
7	//!   array between them: `seq[pos]` publishes each slot
8	//!   independently, because claim order and commit order
9	//!   differ under concurrent producers.
10	//! - Producers CAS-claim `producer_idx`; the consumer stays
11	//!   CAS-free. Fullness is read from the slot seq, so
12	//!   producers never load `consumer_idx`.
13	//! - Own magic and layout version: a region is one kind or
14	//!   the other, and cross-attaching fails toward
15	//!   [`Error::BadMagic`].
16	//! - The claim CAS raises the atomic floor for MPSC users
17	//!   only — this module is gated on `target_has_atomic =
18	//!   "32"`; the SPSC ring stays load/store-only.
19	
20	use core::marker::PhantomData;
21	use core::mem::size_of;
22	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
23	
24	use crate::{CACHE_LINE_SIZE, CacheAligned, Error, USER_WORDS};
25	
26	mod consumer;
27	mod producer;
28	
29	pub use consumer::{MpscConsumer, MpscReadSlot};
30	pub use producer::MpscProducer;
31	
32	/// Layout marker written by [`MpscRing::init`]; distinct from
33	/// the SPSC magic so cross-kind attach fails toward
34	/// [`Error::BadMagic`].
35	const MPSC_MAGIC: u32 = 0x5A43_4D31; // "ZCM1"
36	
37	/// Bumped on any change to the MPSC region layout;
38	/// independent of the SPSC layout version.
39	const MPSC_LAYOUT_VERSION: u32 = 1;
40	
41	/// Capacity bound: `2^30`, not the SPSC `2^31` — the seq
42	/// tombstone encoding reserves index-arithmetic headroom (see
43	/// the design doc's "MPSC open questions").
44	const MPSC_MAX_CAPACITY: u32 = 1 << 30;
45	
46	/// Tombstone offset: an unwound `send_with` commits
47	/// `pos + 1 + TOMBSTONE` instead of `pos + 1`, marking a
48	/// claimed slot the consumer must release without delivering.
49	///
50	/// - Unambiguous at both wait points: the consumer at `c`
51	///   legitimately sees only `seq - (c+1)` of `-1` (not yet
52	///   committed) or `0` (committed), so exactly `2^31` means
53	///   tombstone; a producer's wrapping-signed diff reads a
54	///   tombstoned previous lap as negative — "full" — until the
55	///   consumer skips it, which is the correct backpressure.
56	pub(crate) const TOMBSTONE: u32 = 1 << 31;
57	
58	/// Control block at offset 0 of an MPSC region — same
59	/// four-line shape as the SPSC [`Header`](crate::Header), but
60	/// its own type: the layouts evolve independently and the
61	/// index-ownership story differs.
62	///
63	/// - line 0: geometry, written by [`MpscRing::init`] with
64	///   `magic` last (`Release`), read-only thereafter.
65	/// - line 1: `producer_idx` — CAS-claimed by *all* producers
66	///   (the contended line), never read by the consumer's hot
67	///   path.
68	/// - line 2: `consumer_idx` — written only by the consumer;
69	///   never read by producers (fullness comes from the slot
70	///   seq), so it is resume state and diagnostic occupancy.
71	/// - line 3: `user`, app-owned scratch — same contract as the
72	///   SPSC user line.
73	/// - Every field is atomic: the region may be mapped by a
74	///   peer at any time.
75	#[repr(C)]
76	pub struct MpscHeader {
77	    /// Layout marker ([`MPSC_MAGIC`]); stored last by init
78	    /// (`Release`), loaded first by attach (`Acquire`).
79	    magic: AtomicU32,
80	    /// Layout version ([`MPSC_LAYOUT_VERSION`]).
81	    layout_version: AtomicU32,
82	    /// Slot size N in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] multiple.
83	    slot_size: AtomicU32,
84	    /// Slot count M — a power of two `<= 2^30`.
85	    capacity: AtomicU32,
86	    /// [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] this region was built with.
87	    cache_line_size: AtomicU32,
88	    /// Free-running count of slot positions claimed by
89	    /// producers (CAS).
90	    producer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,
91	    /// Free-running count of messages released; consumer-owned
92	    /// resume state, not read by producers.
93	    consumer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,
94	    /// App-owned scratch line ([`USER_WORDS`] words): zeroed by
95	    /// init, then never touched by the crate.
96	    user: CacheAligned<[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS]>,
97	}
98	
99	const _: () = assert!(size_of::<MpscHeader>() == 4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE);
100	
101	/// A validated view over an MPSC ring region.
102	///
103	/// - Geometry is snapshotted out of the header at init/attach,
104	///   as the SPSC ring does: per-op paths never re-read fields
105	///   a peer could scribble on.
106	/// - Region layout: [`MpscHeader`], the seq array (`M ×
107	///   AtomicU32`, padded up to a cache line), then M slots of N
108	///   bytes.
109	pub struct MpscRing<'a> {
110	    /// The region's control block.
111	    header: &'a MpscHeader,
112	    /// Base of the per-slot sequence array.
113	    seqs: *const AtomicU32,
114	    /// Base of the slot array.
115	    slots: *mut u8,
116	    /// Snapshot of `header.slot_size`.
117	    slot_size: u32,
118	    /// Snapshot of `header.capacity`.
119	    capacity: u32,
120	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
121	    mask: u32,
122	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
123	}
124	
125	impl<'a> MpscRing<'a> {
126	    /// Initialize a fresh region and return the ring over it.
127	    ///
128	    /// - `slot_size` — N bytes per slot, a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]
129	    ///   multiple.
130	    /// - `capacity` — M slots, a power of two `<= 2^30`.
131	    /// - The region must be [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]-aligned and at
132	    ///   least [`mpsc_region_size`] bytes.
133	    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {
134	        validate_mpsc_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
135	        let len = region.len();
136	        // Taken exactly once — see `Ring::init` for the Stacked
137	        // Borrows rationale.
138	        let base = region.as_mut_ptr();
139	        let header = mpsc_header_ptr(base, len)?;
140	        if (len as u64) < mpsc_region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
141	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
142	        }
143	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the MpscHeader checked
144	        // by mpsc_header_ptr; region is exclusively borrowed
145	        // for 'a; any byte pattern is a valid MpscHeader
146	        // (all-atomic fields, plain-byte padding).
147	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
148	        header
149	            .layout_version
150	            .store(MPSC_LAYOUT_VERSION, Ordering::Relaxed);
151	        header.slot_size.store(slot_size, Ordering::Relaxed);
152	        header.capacity.store(capacity, Ordering::Relaxed);
153	        header
154	            .cache_line_size
155	            .store(CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, Ordering::Relaxed);
156	        header.producer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
157	        header.consumer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
158	        for word in header.user.iter() {
159	            word.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
160	        }
161	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len covers header + seq array.
162	        let seqs = unsafe { base.add(size_of::<MpscHeader>()) } as *const AtomicU32;
163	        // seq[i] = i marks every slot claimable by the position
164	        // that will first reach it.
165	        for i in 0..capacity {
166	            // SAFETY: i < capacity, inside the seq array
167	            // validated by the region-size check.
168	            unsafe { &*seqs.add(i as usize) }.store(i, Ordering::Relaxed);
169	        }
170	        // Published last: a peer that pre-mapped the region must
171	        // never observe the magic before the geometry and seq
172	        // stores it validates and relies on.
173	        header.magic.store(MPSC_MAGIC, Ordering::Release);
174	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len covers header + seqs + slots.
175	        let slots = unsafe { base.add(slots_offset(capacity)) };
176	        Ok(MpscRing {
177	            header,
178	            seqs,
179	            slots,
180	            slot_size,
181	            capacity,
182	            mask: capacity - 1,
183	            _region: PhantomData,
184	        })
185	    }
186	
187	    /// Attach to a region another process (or an earlier call)
188	    /// already initialized, validating its header.
189	    ///
190	    /// # Safety
191	    ///
192	    /// - `region` points to `len` bytes of memory that outlive
193	    ///   `'a`, genuinely shared and writable (e.g. a
194	    ///   `MAP_SHARED` mapping).
195	    /// - At most one consumer attaches; any number of
196	    ///   producers may (MPSC contract).
197	    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {
198	        let header = mpsc_header_ptr(region, len)?;
199	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the MpscHeader checked
200	        // by mpsc_header_ptr; caller guarantees the memory is
201	        // live and shared.
202	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
203	        // Acquire pairs with init's Release store of magic: a
204	        // valid magic means the geometry and seq stores are
205	        // visible.
206	        if header.magic.load(Ordering::Acquire) != MPSC_MAGIC {
207	            return Err(Error::BadMagic);
208	        }
209	        if header.layout_version.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != MPSC_LAYOUT_VERSION {
210	            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);
211	        }
212	        if header.cache_line_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32 {
213	            return Err(Error::BadCacheLine);
214	        }
215	        // Snapshot geometry once; per-op paths never re-read it.
216	        let slot_size = header.slot_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
217	        let capacity = header.capacity.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
218	        validate_mpsc_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
219	        if (len as u64) < mpsc_region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
220	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
221	        }
222	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len covers header + seqs + slots.
223	        let seqs = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<MpscHeader>()) } as *const AtomicU32;
224	        // SAFETY: as above.
225	        let slots = unsafe { region.add(slots_offset(capacity)) };
226	        Ok(MpscRing {
227	            header,
228	            seqs,
229	            slots,
230	            slot_size,
231	            capacity,
232	            mask: capacity - 1,
233	            _region: PhantomData,
234	        })
235	    }
236	
237	    /// Split into one producer handle and the consumer handle.
238	    ///
239	    /// - The producer is `Clone`: one clone per producing
240	    ///   thread (MPSC contract). The consumer is unique per
241	    ///   process; cross-process, exactly one consuming process
242	    ///   is the caller's contract (see [`MpscRing::attach`]).
243	    pub fn split(self) -> (MpscProducer<'a>, MpscConsumer<'a>) {
244	        (
245	            MpscProducer::new(
246	                self.header,
247	                self.seqs,
248	                self.slots,
249	                self.slot_size,
250	                self.mask,
251	            ),
252	            MpscConsumer::new(
253	                self.header,
254	                self.seqs,
255	                self.slots,
256	                self.slot_size,
257	                self.capacity,
258	                self.mask,
259	            ),
260	        )
261	    }
262	}
263	
264	/// Validate a region base pointer and cast it to the
265	/// [`MpscHeader`] it must start with; shared by init / attach.
266	///
267	/// - Same split of duties as the SPSC `header_ptr`: alignment
268	///   and header room here, full-geometry length with the
269	///   caller.
270	fn mpsc_header_ptr(base: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<*const MpscHeader, Error> {
271	    if !(base as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE) {
272	        return Err(Error::Misaligned);
273	    }
274	    if len < size_of::<MpscHeader>() {
275	        return Err(Error::TooSmall);
276	    }
277	    Ok(base as *const MpscHeader)
278	}
279	
280	/// Bytes the seq array occupies, padded up to a cache line so
281	/// the slot array behind it stays line-aligned.
282	///
283	/// - u64: `capacity * 4` reaches `2^32` at the `2^30` cap,
284	///   which would wrap a 32-bit usize.
285	fn seq_bytes(capacity: u32) -> u64 {
286	    (capacity as u64 * size_of::<AtomicU32>() as u64).next_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u64)
287	}
288	
289	/// Byte offset of the slot array: header, then the padded seq
290	/// array.
291	///
292	/// - usize return: callers offset a pointer with it, and only
293	///   after the u64 region-size check has proven the region —
294	///   hence this offset — fits in memory.
295	fn slots_offset(capacity: u32) -> usize {
296	    size_of::<MpscHeader>() + seq_bytes(capacity) as usize
297	}
298	
299	/// Bytes needed for an MPSC region with the given geometry.
300	///
301	/// - Computed in u64 for the same 32-bit wrap reason as the
302	///   SPSC `region_size`.
303	pub fn mpsc_region_size(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> u64 {
304	    size_of::<MpscHeader>() as u64 + seq_bytes(capacity) + slot_size as u64 * capacity as u64
305	}
306	
307	/// Geometry checks for [`MpscRing::init`] / [`MpscRing::attach`].
308	///
309	/// - Slot size as the SPSC ring; capacity additionally capped
310	///   at [`MPSC_MAX_CAPACITY`] for tombstone headroom.
311	fn validate_mpsc_geometry(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {
312	    if slot_size == 0 || !(slot_size as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE_SIZE) {
313	        return Err(Error::BadSlotSize);
314	    }
315	    if capacity == 0 || !capacity.is_power_of_two() || capacity > MPSC_MAX_CAPACITY {
316	        return Err(Error::BadCapacity);
317	    }
318	    Ok(())
319	}
320	
321	#[cfg(test)]
322	mod tests {
323	    use super::*;
324	    use crate::Ring;
325	
326	    /// Test region: header + seq line (4 × 4 B padded to 64) +
327	    /// 4 slots × 1 line.
328	    const REGION_BYTES: usize = size_of::<MpscHeader>() + CACHE_LINE_SIZE + (4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE);
329	
330	    /// Cache-line-aligned backing store for the tests' rings.
331	    #[repr(C, align(64))]
332	    struct Region([u8; REGION_BYTES]);
333	
334	    impl Region {
335	        fn new() -> Self {
336	            Region([0; REGION_BYTES])
337	        }
338	    }
339	
340	    #[test]
341	    fn mpsc_region_size_accounts_for_seq_array() {
342	        // 4 slots: header 256 + seq 16→64 + slots 256 = 576.
343	        assert_eq!(mpsc_region_size(64, 4), 576);
344	        // 32 slots: seq 128 is already a line multiple.
345	        assert_eq!(
346	            mpsc_region_size(64, 32),
347	            (size_of::<MpscHeader>() + 128 + 32 * 64) as u64
348	        );
349	    }
350	
351	    #[test]
352	    fn mpsc_init_rejects_bad_geometry() {
353	        let mut r = Region::new();
354	        assert_eq!(
355	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 63, 4).err().unwrap(),
356	            Error::BadSlotSize
357	        );
358	        assert_eq!(
359	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 0, 4).err().unwrap(),
360	            Error::BadSlotSize
361	        );
362	        assert_eq!(
363	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 3).err().unwrap(),
364	            Error::BadCapacity
365	        );
366	        assert_eq!(
367	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 0).err().unwrap(),
368	            Error::BadCapacity
369	        );
370	        // Over the MPSC cap (fine for SPSC): tombstone headroom.
371	        assert_eq!(
372	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 1 << 31).err().unwrap(),
373	            Error::BadCapacity
374	        );
375	        assert_eq!(
376	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 8).err().unwrap(),
377	            Error::TooSmall
378	        );
379	        assert_eq!(
380	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0[1..], 64, 4).err().unwrap(),
381	            Error::Misaligned
382	        );
383	        // 32-bit tripwire, as the SPSC test: N * M wraps a
384	        // 32-bit usize; the u64 region size must still reject.
385	        assert_eq!(
386	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 1 << 26, 1 << 6).err().unwrap(),
387	            Error::TooSmall
388	        );
389	        // An SPSC-sized region (no seq line) is too small here.
390	        let spsc_bytes = size_of::<crate::Header>() + 4 * 64;
391	        assert_eq!(
392	            MpscRing::init(&mut r.0[..spsc_bytes], 64, 4).err().unwrap(),
393	            Error::TooSmall
394	        );
395	    }

398	    fn mpsc_init_seeds_seq_array() {
399	        let mut r = Region::new();
400	        // Dirty the region so the seq stores are observable.
401	        r.0.fill(0xAA);
402	        let ring = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
403	        for i in 0..4 {
404	            // SAFETY: i < capacity; test mirrors init's bounds.
405	            let seq = unsafe { &*ring.seqs.add(i as usize) };
406	            assert_eq!(seq.load(Ordering::Relaxed), i);
407	        }
408	        assert_eq!(ring.slot_size, 64);
409	        assert_eq!(ring.capacity, 4);
410	        assert_eq!(ring.mask, 3);

428	            .err()

451	    use crate::{Empty, Full};
452	    use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
453	
454	    /// Test message; two words so a torn write would be
455	    /// visible, `val` doubles as the producer id in threaded
456	    /// tests.
457	    #[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable, Debug, PartialEq)]
458	    #[repr(C)]
459	    struct Msg {
460	        seq: u64,
461	        val: u64,
462	    }
463	
464	    #[test]
465	    fn mpsc_roundtrip_full_empty() {
466	        let mut r = Region::new();
467	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
468	
469	        // Empty at start.
470	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
471	
472	        // Fill all 4 slots, then Full on a single probe.
473	        for i in 0..4u64 {
474	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(
475	                |_| false,
476	                |m| {
477	                    m.seq = i;
478	                    m.val = i * 10;
479	                },
480	            )
481	            .unwrap();
482	        }
483	        assert_eq!(
484	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |_| {}).err().unwrap(),
485	            Full
486	        );
487	
488	        // Drain in order; then empty again.
489	        for i in 0..4u64 {
490	            let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
491	            assert_eq!(
492	                *msg,
493	                Msg {
494	                    seq: i,
495	                    val: i * 10
496	                }
497	            );
498	            msg.release();
499	        }
500	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
501	
502	        // One more send lands in the masked-around slot 0.
503	        prod.send_with::<Msg>(
504	            |_| false,
505	            |m| {
506	                m.seq = 4;
507	                m.val = 0;
508	            },
509	        )
510	        .unwrap();
511	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
512	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 4);
513	        msg.release();
514	    }
515	
516	    #[test]
517	    fn mpsc_policies_count_and_give_up() {
518	        let mut r = Region::new();
519	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
520	
521	        // Empty ring: the consumer's policy sees 0-based
522	        // attempts and its `false` becomes Err(Empty).
523	        let mut seen = Vec::new();
524	        let err = cons
525	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|attempt| {
526	                seen.push(attempt);
527	                attempt < 2
528	            })
529	            .err()
530	            .unwrap();
531	        assert_eq!(err, Empty);
532	        assert_eq!(seen, [0, 1, 2]);
533	
534	        // Fill the ring: the producer's policy gives up with
535	        // Err(Full), and the failed send has zero footprint —
536	        // the fill closure never ran.
537	        for i in 0..4u64 {
538	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = i).unwrap();
539	        }
540	        let mut seen = Vec::new();
541	        let err = prod
542	            .send_with::<Msg>(
543	                |attempt| {
544	                    seen.push(attempt);
545	                    attempt < 2
546	                },
547	                |_| panic!("fill ran on a full ring"),
548	            )
549	            .err()
550	            .unwrap();
551	        assert_eq!(err, Full);
552	        assert_eq!(seen, [0, 1, 2]);
553	
554	        // With room / messages available the policy is never
555	        // consulted.
556	        let msg = cons
557	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| panic!("policy consulted with a message available"))
558	            .unwrap();
559	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 0);
560	        msg.release();
561	        prod.send_with::<Msg>(
562	            |_| panic!("policy consulted with room available"),
563	            |m| m.seq = 4,
564	        )
565	        .unwrap();
566	    }
567	
568	    #[test]
569	    // Dropping the guard is the behavior under test.
570	    #[allow(clippy::drop_non_drop)]
571	    fn mpsc_abandoned_read_guard_redelivers() {
572	        let mut r = Region::new();
573	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
574	
575	        prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = 1).unwrap();
576	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
577	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
578	        drop(msg);
579	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
580	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
581	        msg.release();
582	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
583	    }
584	
585	    #[test]
586	    fn mpsc_panicking_fill_tombstones_and_skips() {
587	        let mut r = Region::new();
588	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
589	
590	        // A committed message, a panicked fill, another
591	        // committed message.
592	        prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = 1).unwrap();
593	        let unwound = std::panic::catch_unwind(std::panic::AssertUnwindSafe(|| {
594	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(
595	                |_| false,
596	                |m| {
597	                    m.seq = 99; // partially filled, then…
598	                    panic!("fill panics");
599	                },
600	            )
601	        }));
602	        assert!(unwound.is_err());
603	        prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = 2).unwrap();
604	
605	        // The consumer sees 1 then 2; the tombstoned slot is
606	        // released without delivery.
607	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
608	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
609	        msg.release();
610	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
611	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 2);
612	        msg.release();
613	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
614	
615	        // The skipped slot is claimable again: a full lap
616	        // still fits 4 messages.
617	        for i in 3..7u64 {
618	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = i).unwrap();
619	        }
620	        assert!(prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |_| {}).is_err());
621	        for i in 3..7u64 {
622	            let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
623	            assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
624	            msg.release();
625	        }
626	    }
627	
628	    #[test]
629	    fn mpsc_indices_survive_u32_wrap() {
630	        let mut r = Region::new();
631	        let ring = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
632	        // Simulate a long-running ring two commits shy of the
633	        // u32 wrap: indices at MAX-1, and each slot's seq must
634	        // equal the free-running position that will next claim
635	        // it (positions MAX-1, MAX, 0, 1 → slots 2, 3, 0, 1).
636	        let start = u32::MAX - 1;
637	        ring.header.producer_idx.store(start, Ordering::Relaxed);
638	        ring.header.consumer_idx.store(start, Ordering::Relaxed);
639	        for (slot, pos) in [(2u32, u32::MAX - 1), (3, u32::MAX), (0, 0), (1, 1)] {
640	            // SAFETY: slot < capacity; test mirrors init.
641	            unsafe { &*ring.seqs.add(slot as usize) }.store(pos, Ordering::Relaxed);
642	        }
643	        let (prod, mut cons) = ring.split();
644	
645	        // Fill across the wrap, then Full.
646	        for i in 0..4u64 {
647	            prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |m| m.seq = i).unwrap();
648	        }
649	        assert!(prod.send_with::<Msg>(|_| false, |_| {}).is_err());
650	
651	        // Drain in order across the wrap.
652	        for i in 0..4u64 {
653	            let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).unwrap();
654	            assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
655	            msg.release();
656	        }
657	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
658	    }
659	
660	    #[test]
661	    fn threaded_mpsc_two_producers() {
662	        // Reduced under Miri: interpreted spin loops are slow.
663	        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 100 } else { 50_000 };
664	        const PRODUCERS: u64 = 2;
665	        let mut r = Region::new();
666	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
667	
668	        std::thread::scope(|s| {
669	            for p in 0..PRODUCERS {
670	                let prod = prod.clone();
671	                s.spawn(move || {
672	                    for i in 0..COUNT {
673	                        prod.send_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin, |m| {
674	                            m.seq = i;
675	                            m.val = p;
676	                        })
677	                        .unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
678	                    }
679	                });
680	            }
681	            s.spawn(move || {
682	                // Global arrival order is claim order; only
683	                // per-producer FIFO is promised.
684	                let mut next = [0u64; PRODUCERS as usize];
685	                for _ in 0..PRODUCERS * COUNT {
686	                    let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin).unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
687	                    let p = msg.val as usize;
688	                    assert_eq!(msg.seq, next[p], "per-producer order broken");
689	                    next[p] += 1;
690	                    msg.release();
691	                }
692	                assert!(cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| false).is_err());
693	            });
694	        });
695	    }
696	
697	    #[test]
698	    fn threaded_mpsc_shared_reference_producers() {
699	        // The Sync path: two threads share one &MpscProducer
700	        // instead of cloning.
701	        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 100 } else { 50_000 };
702	        let mut r = Region::new();
703	        let (prod, mut cons) = MpscRing::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
704	        let prod = &prod;
705	
706	        std::thread::scope(|s| {
707	            for p in 0..2u64 {
708	                s.spawn(move || {
709	                    for i in 0..COUNT {
710	                        prod.send_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin, |m| {
711	                            m.seq = i;
712	                            m.val = p;
713	                        })
714	                        .unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
715	                    }
716	                });
717	            }
718	            s.spawn(move || {
719	                let mut next = [0u64; 2];
720	                for _ in 0..2 * COUNT {
721	                    let msg = cons.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(crate::policy::spin).unwrap(); // OK: policy::spin never gives up
722	                    let p = msg.val as usize;
723	                    assert_eq!(msg.seq, next[p]);
724	                    next[p] += 1;
725	                    msg.release();
726	                }
727	            });
728	        });
729	    }

1	# Cycle protocol
2	
3	This protocol uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
4	contains instructions on how a commit cycle is accomplished.
5	
6	The artifact a cycle produces is whatever the bot generates from
7	the conversation — code, prose, an image, a song, a screenplay.
8	The steps below use a Rust crate as the running example (the
9	cargo cycle, `Cargo.toml` versioning); substitute your medium's
10	equivalents — this project's manifest is recorded in
11	[versioning.md](versioning.md).
12	
13	## Cycles
14	
15	A cycle has three phases:
16	
17	- **[Preparation](#preparation)** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's
18	  first commit, when it needs setup (a lightweight cycle omits
19	  it and starts at `-1` — see
20	  [versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)). Sets up the
21	  cycle:
22	  - Bump the version-of-record to `X.Y.Z-0` (where it lives
23	    and the suffix scheme are project-specific — see
24	    [versioning.md](versioning.md)).
25	  - Pick up a `## Todo` item (typically the top-ranked,
26	    #1) into `## In Progress` (bold title + succinct problem
27	    statement + plan ladder).
28	  - Open the [chores section](#chores-sections).
29	- **[Work-N](#work-n)** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the
30	  commits that implement the change. As many as the change
31	  needs; each runs through the
32	  [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
33	- **[Close-out](#close-out)** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's
34	  last commit. Bookkeeping only:
35	  - Move the picked-up item to a one-line entry in
36	    `## Done`.
37	  - Move the `## In Progress` block into the
38	    [chores section](#chores-sections).
39	  - Optionally update `notes/README.md` if functionality
40	    changed.
41	
42	A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
43	either incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the
44	result must always be published at close-out. See
45	[Pushing](#pushing).
46	
47	**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to
48	warrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
49	Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
50	Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
51	at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
52	suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
53	for the local-ladder mechanics.
54	
55	## Chores sections
56	
57	A **chores section** is a `##` section in
58	`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording landed work. In
59	general, every commit that lands on the permanent branch
60	should have a reference to it on a `Commits:` list in a
61	chores file.
62	
63	The phrase **"Open" the chores section** means append a
64	`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
65	with the title it records (e.g. `## refactor: foo bar`),
66	followed by an **empty `Commits:`** line. The narrative
67	body stays empty until close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out));
68	the `Commits:` line is backfilled later, once the commit is
69	permanent (see [Commits backfill](#commits-backfill) below).
70	
71	Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
72	design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live in
73	AGENTS.md [Chores conventions](../AGENTS.md#chores-conventions).
74	
75	### Commits backfill
76	
77	A chores section's `Commits:` line cites the commit(s) it
78	records, by SHA — but a SHA isn't stable until the commit
79	lands on a **permanent branch** (`main`, or a long-lived
80	release/patch branch that won't be rewritten); a rebase or
81	squash rewrites it on the way. So:
82	
83	- A chores section is **opened with an empty `Commits:`** line.
84	- **Backfill once the commit is on a permanent branch**,
85	  where its SHA is final. A commit can't record its own SHA
86	  (that would change the hash), so the fill always lands one
87	  push later: **each push backfills the `Commits:` of the
88	  commits the previous push made permanent.** On a topic
89	  branch the sections instead wait until the branch lands —
90	  so no SHA is ever written that a later rebase could
91	  invalidate.
92	
93	Use `[[N]]` refs — several as `[[N]],[[M]]` only when one
94	section records multiple commits (a merge non-ff close-out) —
95	with the commit URL + 40-hex SHA in the file's `# References`
96	(format in AGENTS.md
97	[Chores commit references](../AGENTS.md#chores-commit-references)).
98	A section's `##` title matches its commit title, so a rare
99	deliberate rewrite of a permanent-branch commit re-syncs via
100	`git log --grep "<title>"`.
101	
102	The per-push cadence is a project choice, not dogma — a
103	**per-close-out** model (recording a cycle's SHAs at its
104	close-out) is equally valid. The one invariant: a recorded
105	SHA must be permanent.
106	
107	## Preparation
108	
109	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`), when the cycle needs
110	setup (a lightweight cycle omits it — see
111	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)):
112	
113	- **Bump the version-of-record** to `X.Y.Z-0`. Where it
114	  lives, the suffix scheme, and any derived files (a
115	  lockfile, a sourced manifest version) are
116	  project-specific — see [versioning.md](versioning.md).
117	- **Move a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one) into
118	  `## In Progress` and the todo item should have:
119	  - A **bold title line** — that will be the chores
120	    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
121	  - A **succinct problem statement**; add if one is needed
122	  - A **plan ladder**.
123	- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
124	  append a `##` header with the title it records — the
125	  cycle's anticipated close-out title.
126	
127	## Work-N
128	
129	The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
130	implement the change. As many as needed:
131	
132	- Each commit runs through the
133	  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
134	- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
135	  visibility).
136	- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
137	  [Pushing](#pushing)).
138	- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
139	  scope grows enough (see
140	  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).
141	
142	## Close-out
143	
144	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
145	only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
146	what happens post-squash:
147	
148	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
149	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
150	- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
151	  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
152	  placeholder opened during Preparation):
153	  - Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
154	    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
155	    chores `##` header already carries that string).
156	  - Paste under the chores `##` header.
157	  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
158	    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
159	    back-reference.
160	  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
161	    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
162	  - Replace `## In Progress` with
163	    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
164	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
165	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
166	
167	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
168	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
169	see [Pushing](#pushing).
170	
171	## Numbering
172	
173	Each commit's phase is encoded in the version suffix — `-0`
174	Preparation, `-1`/`-2`/… Work, bare `X.Y.Z` Close-out,
175	recursively for sub-cycles. The full scheme — disambiguation,
176	nesting, optional Preparation, the project's version-of-record
177	format, and the per-phase bump — lives in
178	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering), which is the
179	single source of truth for this repo's versioning.
180	
181	## Per-commit flow
182	
183	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
184	through:
185	
186	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
187	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`.
188	2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
189	   for the loop-and-squash technique).
190	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
191	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit.
192	4. **Validate the artifact** — a medium-specific step, skip-able
193	   for notes-only commits, mandatory at close-out. For the Rust
194	   example the cargo cycle is:
195	   1. `cargo fmt`
196	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
197	   3. `cargo test`
198	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
199	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
200	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
201	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
202	   changes and we iterate until complete.
203	6. **Write the commit description** — see
204	   [Commit description](#commit-description).
205	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
206	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
207	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
208	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
209	   keeps the verb visible):
210	
211	   ```
212	   jj commit -m \
213	   "<type>: <short description>" \
214	   -m "<intro paragraph>
215	
216	   - file1: gist
217	   - file2: gist
218	
219	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
220	   -R .
221	   ```
222	
223	**Two overrides apply:**
224	
225	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
226	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
227	  surface it; don't push through.
228	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
229	  a review or question forward.
230	
231	## Commit description
232	
233	[Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/):
234	
235	```
236	<type>: <short description>
237	```
238	
239	Titles carry **no trailing `(<version>)` suffix**. The
240	version-of-record — where it lives and its bump cadence, see
241	[versioning.md](versioning.md) — is useful for confirming
242	you're running the version you're testing, not a per-commit
243	title marker. We think per-commit version-in-title is
244	unstable on large projects: many changes are in flight
245	simultaneously, and a
246	version is only stable once merged into the main repo — so
247	titles don't carry one.
248	
249	### Title
250	
251	- ≤50 chars total.
252	- Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`,
253	  `docs`, `chore`.
254	- Favor terse phrasings.
255	- **Distinct per step** — each of a cycle's commits gets its
256	  own descriptive title (no shared cycle title with a step
257	  marker). Share a greppable stem across the cycle's titles
258	  (e.g. `ring buffer`) so `git log --grep` collects them; the
259	  chores section header matches the close-out title. See
260	  AGENTS.md
261	  [Conventional-commit shape](../AGENTS.md#conventional-commit-shape-ladder--chores--commit).
262	
263	### Body
264	
265	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form) (intro + bullets),
266	wrap ≤72. Bullet content differs per repo:
267	
268	- **App-repo body**: file-by-file. One bullet per file
269	  changed (file plus a one-line gist). Sub-bullets for
270	  files with multiple distinct changes:
271	
272	  ```
273	  - path/to/file1
274	    - first distinct change
275	    - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
276	       with continuation indented 5
277	  ```
278	
279	  The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
280	  cycle's mechanical change record. Chores carries the
281	  narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
282	  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.
283	
284	- **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: bullets describe
285	  in-session activity rather than code changes.
286	
287	### Trailer
288	
289	`ochid:` as the last line of the body — see
290	[Cross-repo linking (ochid trailers)](../AGENTS.md#cross-repo-linking-ochid-trailers)
291	in AGENTS.md for the convention.
292	
293	For breaking changes, use the hyphenated `BREAKING-CHANGE:`
294	trailer key. `BREAKING CHANGE:` (with a space) is the only
295	space-separated key the Conventional Commits spec allows; the
296	hyphenated form is also valid and avoids the space ambiguity.
297	
298	## Reviewing changes
299	
300	Work review looks at the **uncommitted working-copy diff**,
301	on the way to commit. The user opens diffs in their
302	editor (Zed, VSCode); jj commands are for terminal:
303	
304	- `jj diff` — working-copy diff (uncommitted)
305	- `jj diff -r @-` — diff of the previous commit
306	- `jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>` — any two revisions
307	- `jj show -r <X>` — description + diff for one rev
308	
309	Don't `jj edit -r @-` to view a past commit — that marks
310	it mutable and shifts `@`; use `jj diff -r @-` or
311	`jj show -r @-`.
312	
313	See [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) for the
314	close-out squash recipe and recovery; revset primitives
315	are in [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets).
316	
317	## Pushing
318	
319	### Policy
320	
321	Push is **discretionary** during the cycle (backup,
322	progress visibility) and **mandatory at close-out** —
323	the cycle's result must be published.
324	
325	**Approval is per-push.** Every push — any repo, any kind:
326	cycle push, interim backup, recovery/surgery force-push —
327	happens only after the user has reviewed the changes to be
328	published and explicitly approved that specific push.
329	Approval of a plan that *includes* a push does not authorize
330	the push itself; stop and ask again at the moment of pushing.
331	
332	**Default is interactive; an explicit scoped delegation waives
333	the gates.** The gates above — per-push approval, the
334	commit-description review (show title+body and stop), and the
335	hard stop after push/finalize — are the *interactive default*.
336	They yield when the user **explicitly** delegates a complete,
337	bounded task and authorizes carrying it through ("do all of X
338	and push each step, don't check in"). The bot then proceeds
339	through that task's commits and pushes without stopping, and
340	continues past each push to the next step. Conditions:
341	
342	- **Explicit grant** — never inferred from a task merely being
343	  well-scoped; the user's words must authorize unattended
344	  completion. "Commit and push" (or "then push") names the
345	  destination, not a waiver: it authorizes the push *after*
346	  the normal work review and description review, not skipping
347	  them. Only wording that explicitly waives the stops ("don't
348	  check in", "no need to review", "carry it through
349	  unattended") waives them.
350	- **Bounded goal** — covers the named task only; does not carry
351	  to the next task or a vaguer follow-on.
352	- **Destructive ops still pause** — delegation covers the task's
353	  ordinary commits and pushes; it does *not* pre-authorize a
354	  genuinely irreversible action (force-push over published
355	  history, history rewrite, deleting a remote branch). Those can
356	  permanently destroy work and aren't a normal cycle step, so the
357	  bot flags one before acting. An ordinary delegated cycle never
358	  reaches this.
359	- **Still transparent** — report each commit/push as it lands
360	  (title + outcome) so the user can catch up.
361	- **When in doubt, ask** — ambiguous authorization falls back to
362	  per-push approval.
363	
364	### Shape at close-out push
365	
366	At close-out the cycle's *work* is done; its *published
367	shape* is the remaining choice, made at push time. Surface
368	the options and get user approval before pushing — once on
369	the target, changing shape is a remote rewrite (force-push,
370	needs approval), so choose deliberately.
371	
372	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target.
373	  Right for straightforward changes where the Work-N is
374	  focused on one or two files.
375	- **Merge non-ff** *(current default)* — `main` gains a
376	  merge commit (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via
377	  two parents. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
378	  shape. See [Merge non-ff recipe](#merge-non-ff-recipe)
379	  for the full setup sequence.
380	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
381	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
382	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
383	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
384	
385	Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use
386	`jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes.
387	
388	### Merge non-ff recipe
389	
390	Setting up a [Merge non-ff](#shape-at-close-out-push)
391	close-out is a fixed sequence. `<closeout>` is the cycle's
392	close-out commit, `<prev>` the previous cycle's close-out
393	(the current `main` tip), `<work-tip>` the cycle's last
394	Work commit:
395	
396	1. **Rebase the close-out into a merge** — `jj rebase -r
397	   <closeout> --onto <prev> --onto <work-tip>`.
398	   - `-r <closeout>` keeps the `<closeout>` commit in place.
399	   - `--onto <prev>` becomes its first parent (trunk).
400	   - `--onto <work-tip>` becomes the second parent.
401	
402	   Together these make `<closeout>` a merge of
403	   `<prev>` + `<work-tip>`, forming a trapezoidal commit.
404	2. **Use `jj new <merge>`** to add an empty `@` above the
405	   merge. The rebase left `@` *on* the now-content-bearing
406	   merge, which git/IDE diff views show as uncommitted;
407	   `jj new` restores the clean empty `@` on top.
408	3. **Push** — `jj git push --bookmark main -R .`.
409	
410	**Post-hoc caveat.** If the cycle was already pushed
411	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) its commits are
412	immutable: the rebase needs `--ignore-immutable` and the
413	push force-updates `main`.
414	The standard sequence assumes the merge is set up *before*
415	the close-out push.
416	
417	### vc-x1 push wrapper
418	
419	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps per-push mechanics. See
420	`vc-x1 push --help` for current flags.
421	
422	**Current limitation**: only fully supports the
423	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) shape; other
424	shapes need manual jj steps. Improvements tracked /
425	planned:
426	
427	- N:1 code↔bot for Merge non-ff (`## Todo` entry P1).
428	- Symmetric squash (planned, to be captured in `-2`).
429	- Per-repo bookmark names (planned).
430	
431	### .claude cadence
432	
433	**Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired
434	with every code commit in that push.
435	
436	The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data
437	across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across
438	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so
439	code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.
440	
441	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
442	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
443	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
444	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
445	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
446	
447	Ending a session: if the user runs `/exit` there will be
448	session information created, which we don't worry about.
449	The user can close the terminal instead and `@` will
450	remain empty.
451	
452	### Bot communication at the reviews
453	
454	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
455	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
456	
457	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
458	  "Work complete. Please review."
459	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
460	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit/push.
461	  Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push ... --title ...
462	  --body ...` invocation by default.
463	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
464	  options (squash / merge / keep) and the push target;
465	  wait for the user's choice before any `jj squash` /
466	  `jj rebase` / `jj git push` invocation.
467	
468	### After push or finalize: stop and wait
469	
470	After you **push** (cross the remote boundary) or launch
471	**finalize** on the `.claude` repo (`vc-x1 finalize --repo
472	.claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach`) — by
473	hand or via the `vc-x1 push` wrapper — you **MUST NEVER**
474	proceed (next step, edit, tool call, text output) until the
475	user explicitly directs you to continue. **Even when the next
476	step seems obvious — wait.** Treat push-or-finalize as a hard
477	stop for the whole turn.
478	
479	`vc-x1 push` performs both as its tail stages — `push-app`
480	then the detached `vc-x1 finalize` on `.claude` — so there is
481	no gap left to speak in once the push is invoked. Put **all**
482	closing words *before* invoking it. The harness rejects an empty
483	turn, so it may force a visible token after the tool returns; if
484	so, emit a bare acknowledgment only (e.g. "landed") — never a
485	summary, verification, or next-step offer.
486	
487	Why (`.claude` is the live journal finalize snapshots →
488	squashes → pushes):
489	
490	- **`--detach`**: lets the tool return immediately so the
491	  harness can keep flushing the *trailing* session-data — the
492	  tool call's own transcript and any words emitted *before*
493	  the invoke — into the snapshot.
494	- **`--delay 10`**: gives the harness time to complete that
495	  flush before the snapshot is taken. It is **not** time for
496	  the bot to do more work.
497	- **silence**: the bot must not summarize or perform any work
498	  after the tool returns. The harness may force a visible token
499	  (it rejects an empty turn) — keep it to a bare acknowledgment,
500	  never a summary or a next step.
501	
502	**Known slip**: the bot has emitted a summary after launching
503	finalize. There is no "harmless" closing line — if it's worth
504	saying, say it *before* the invoke.
505	
506	### Recovery
507	
508	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
509	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
510	  finalize by hand:
511	
512	  ```
513	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
514	  ```
515	
516	- **Run finalize again if `@` is non-empty** after the
517	  finalize's squash-and-commit (also desirable after
518	  extra activity by the bot's agents).
519	  - Why: finalize snapshots `.claude` at `--delay`, but the
520	    bot keeps writing session data while it runs — the tool's
521	    own invocation plus the bot's closing response land
522	    *after* the snapshot.
523	  - Safe to repeat: bot session data is append-only, so a
524	    re-run never conflicts or overwrites. (This could
525	    change; it is not under the user's control.)
526	  - Fix: run `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push
527	    <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach` another time to capture
528	    that tail; one extra pass is generally enough.
529	  - No guarantees: events outside the bot's control can leave
530	    `@` non-empty — e.g. the bot's back end may decide to
531	    squash/consolidate session data, which can take minutes
532	    and land after the snapshot. The remedy is the same: just
533	    run finalize again. This is why a single pass is never
534	    guaranteed to leave `@` empty.
535	- **Clear push's saved state** after any out-of-band
536	  recovery — `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push
537	  <bookmark> --restart` — otherwise push resumes from a
538	  stale stage.
539	- **Late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded**
540	  (e.g. updating AGENTS.md or memory) requires `jj
541	  squash --ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a
542	  remote rewrite and needs explicit approval like any
543	  push.
544	
545	## Iterative work
546	
547	When work for a single commit (the **target**) benefits
548	from incremental review, loop:
549	
550	1. `jj new -R .` — fresh empty `@` on top of the target.
551	2. Make the next round of changes.
552	3. User reviews the round (see
553	   [Reviewing changes](#reviewing-changes)).
554	4. `jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a
555	   new empty `@`.
556	5. If not done, go to step 2.
557	
558	Same jj mechanics as a
559	[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at
560	single-commit scope — the version
561	doesn't change.
562	
563	## Sub-cycle ladders
564	
565	When a Work commit subdivides into a sub-cycle (see
566	[Numbering](#numbering) for suffix nesting), its Work
567	commits typically live as a local jj `@` chain and
568	**collapse into the sub-cycle's Close-out** before the
569	parent cycle continues. Ladder commits are scratch —
570	for review and bisection only.
571	
572	### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder
573	
574	For each Work commit in the ladder:
575	
576	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
577	2. Do the commit's work.
578	3. Run the fast validation (Rust example: `cargo test
579	   --bins`). **Non-negotiable** — for code, build and clippy
580	   alone miss regressions until a later commit runs the full
581	   suite, raising bisection cost.
582	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — working title
583	   only; the sub-cycle Close-out collects everything
584	   into one final commit.
585	
586	### Navigating the ladder
587	
588	Common moves:
589	
590	- `jj log -r '<base>::' -R .` — see the whole ladder
591	  from its base.
592	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any ladder
593	  commit by chid prefix; useful for bisection.
594	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.
595	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit in
596	  isolation.
597	
598	Modifications to any ladder commit rewrite it in place;
599	descendants auto-rebase.
600	
601	### Close-out: squash the ladder
602	
603	When all ladder Work commits are done and tests pass:
604	
605	```
606	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
607	```
608	
609	`<base>` is the parent of the first ladder commit; `-u`
610	keeps `@`'s description and discards the sources'.
611	After squash, history is linear: `<base> → @`;
612	intermediate commits are auto-abandoned.
613	
614	Then `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` as for any other commit.
615	
616	For N = 1 the squash is a no-op (`<base>..@-` is empty
617	when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit directly.
618	
619	### Recovery
620	
621	If a ladder commit goes wrong, back out without losing
622	prior commits:
623	
624	- **Discard the current commit.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
625	  drops it; you get a fresh empty `@` on the same
626	  parent.
627	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
628	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
629	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
630	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows
631	  the op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts
632	  to that point. Full undo — removes *all* ladder work
633	  after the chosen op. Use only to start over.
634	
635	# References
636	
637	- [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets) — revset primitives
638	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
639	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
640	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
641	  for squash-recipe experiments.
642	- The per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate exists because a
643	  regression introduced in an early ladder commit can go
644	  uncaught until a later commit runs the full suite, raising
645	  bisection cost.
646	
```

1	//! Zero-copy no_std SPSC ring buffer over a caller-provided
2	//! memory region, per [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]:
3	//!
4	//! - One `#[repr(C)]` [`Header`] spanning four cache lines
5	//!   (immutable geometry, producer index, consumer index, app
6	//!   user words), followed by M slots of N bytes.
7	//! - Indices are free-running `AtomicU32`s, masked only at slot
8	//!   access; full is `p - c == M`, no sacrificial slot.
9	//! - Messages move in place: the producer writes through a
10	//!   [`WriteSlot`] `&mut T`, the consumer reads through a
11	//!   [`ReadSlot`] `&T` — zerocopy traits bound `T`, no
12	//!   serialization step. Each side reserves at most one slot at
13	//!   a time; the guard holds the endpoint borrow until commit /
14	//!   release (or drop).
15	//! - The SPSC protocol lives in the `spsc` module; a
16	//!   multi-producer sibling, [`MpscRing`], lives in the
17	//!   `mpsc` module (gated on CAS support) — see its module
18	//!   docs for the claim/seq protocol and closure-send API.
19	//!   Primitive modules hold versioned sibling implementations
20	//!   (`spsc::v0`, …) behind per-module champion aliases; this
21	//!   crate root re-exports the champions.
22	//!
23	//! [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]: https://github.com/winksaville/zc-ring-x1/blob/main/notes/ring-buffer-design.md
24	
25	#![cfg_attr(not(test), no_std)]
26	
27	use core::mem::{align_of, size_of};
28	use core::sync::atomic::AtomicU32;
29	
30	// The MPSC ring needs CAS (the claim), so it is gated; the
31	// SPSC ring protocol stays load/store-only. (The pool's
32	// free-stack also uses CAS and predates the gate — see
33	// notes/bugs.md.)
34	#[cfg(target_has_atomic = "32")]
35	mod mpsc;
36	pub mod policy;
37	mod pool;
38	mod registry;
39	pub mod spsc;
40	
41	#[cfg(target_has_atomic = "32")]
42	pub use mpsc::{MpscConsumer, MpscHeader, MpscProducer, MpscReadSlot, MpscRing, mpsc_region_size};
43	pub use pool::{BufSlot, Exhausted, Pool, PoolHeader, PoolResolver};
44	pub use registry::{Desc, PoolId, PoolRegistry, RegistryError};
45	pub use spsc::{Consumer, Header, Producer, ReadSlot, Ring, WriteSlot};
46	
47	/// Cache-line size the layout is built around.
48	///
49	/// - Slot size must be a multiple of this; slots and the region
50	///   itself must be aligned to it.
51	pub const CACHE_LINE_SIZE: usize = 64;
52	
53	/// Number of `AtomicU32` words in the header's app-owned
54	/// `user` line.
55	pub const USER_WORDS: usize = 16;
56	
57	/// `reserve_slot_with` / `send_with` failed: every slot holds
58	/// an uncommitted-or-unread message.
59	///
60	/// - Shared by the ring primitives (SPSC reserve, MPSC send) —
61	///   defined in the crate core so no primitive depends on a
62	///   sibling's version module for its error type.
63	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
64	pub struct Full;
65	
66	/// `reserve_slot_with` failed: no unread messages.
67	///
68	/// - Shared by the ring primitives' consumer endpoints — see
69	///   [`Full`] for why it lives in the crate core.
70	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
71	pub struct Empty;
72	
73	/// Cache-line-aligned wrapper granting its field sole
74	/// ownership of the line.
75	///
76	/// - `repr(align(N))` accepts only an integer literal — it
77	///   cannot name [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] — so the `64` is written out
78	///   and a const assert ties them back together.
79	#[repr(C, align(64))]
80	struct CacheAligned<T>(T);
81	
82	const _: () = assert!(align_of::<CacheAligned<AtomicU32>>() == CACHE_LINE_SIZE);
83	
84	impl<T> core::ops::Deref for CacheAligned<T> {
85	    type Target = T;
86	    /// Access the wrapped value.
87	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
88	        &self.0
89	    }
90	}
91	
92	/// Errors from region validation — [`Ring::init`] /
93	/// [`Ring::attach`] and [`Pool::init`] / [`Pool::attach`].
94	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
95	pub enum Error {
96	    /// Region is not [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]-aligned.
97	    Misaligned,
98	    /// Region is smaller than header + slots/buffers.
99	    TooSmall,
100	    /// Slot size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`] multiple.
101	    BadSlotSize,
102	    /// Capacity is zero, not a power of two, or `> 2^31`.
103	    BadCapacity,
104	    /// Pool: buffer size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]
105	    /// multiple.
106	    BadBufSize,
107	    /// Pool: buffer count is zero or `u32::MAX` (the
108	    /// free-stack NIL sentinel).
109	    BadBufCount,
110	    /// Attach: magic mismatch — not a region of the expected
111	    /// kind.
112	    BadMagic,
113	    /// Attach: layout version mismatch.
114	    BadLayoutVersion,
115	    /// Attach: region built with a different [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`].
116	    BadCacheLine,
117	}
118	
119	/// Check `T` fits a slot; called once per `reserve_slot_with`
120	/// (both endpoints).
121	///
122	/// - Panics on a type-geometry mismatch — that is a programming
123	///   error, not a runtime condition.
124	fn check_type<T>(slot_size: u32) {
125	    assert!(size_of::<T>() <= slot_size as usize, "T larger than slot");
126	    assert!(
127	        core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE_SIZE,
128	        "T alignment exceeds slot alignment"
129	    );
130	}
131	
132	/// Non-panicking form of [`check_type`], for descriptor
133	/// resolve — there the pool compared against is selected by
134	/// untrusted input, which must not be able to select a panic.
135	fn type_fits<T>(slot_size: u32) -> bool {
136	    size_of::<T>() <= slot_size as usize && core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE_SIZE
137	}
138	
139	/// Pointer to the slot for free-running index `idx`.
140	///
141	/// - Returned range is `slot_size` bytes, cache-line aligned
142	///   (base is, and slot_size is a line multiple).
143	fn slot_ptr(slots: *mut u8, idx: u32, mask: u32, slot_size: u32) -> *mut u8 {
144	    let pos = (idx & mask) as usize;
145	    // SAFETY: pos < capacity, so the offset is inside the slot
146	    // array validated at init/attach.
147	    unsafe { slots.add(pos * slot_size as usize) }
148	}
149	
```

- `src/mpsc/v0/` — the entire MPSC implementation (`mod.rs` with `MpscRing`/`MpscHeader`/tests, `producer.rs`, `consumer.rs`) moved in unchanged; the endpoint files needed no edits since they only import via `super::` and `crate::`.

Ready to commit. Please review.

> DEVELOPER

You use the term champion alias, I've done a search and I don't see that term, can you point me to docs/articles on that terminology

> AGENT

You won't find "champion alias" anywhere — it's a project-local coinage, not established terminology. It entered this repo during the versioning discussion that produced the In Progress decisions block (todo.md's "a champion alias per module (`spsc::Ring = spsc::v0::Ring`)") and then the `spsc/mod.rs` docstring in the previous commit. I carried it forward into `mpsc/mod.rs` for symmetry.

It's a blend of two real things:

- **Champion–challenger** — established terminology in decision science and ML model deployment: the "champion" is the currently-deployed default, "challengers" are candidate replacements run alongside it for comparison, and a winning challenger is promoted to champion. That's the closest documented concept to what the alias does — searching "champion challenger model" or "champion challenger testing" will find plenty (it's long-standing in credit-risk scoring, and cloud ML platforms like SageMaker and Vertex AI use the terms in their deployment docs).
- **Re-export** — the Rust mechanism itself (`pub use v0::Ring;`) is just an ordinary re-export; the Rust reference and API guidelines call it that, sometimes "façade pattern" when a root module re-exports a curated surface.

So the accurate gloss is: "a champion–challenger-style default, implemented as a plain Rust re-export." If you'd rather the docs use only searchable terms, I could reword the docstrings to something like "default-version re-export (champion–challenger style)" — say the word and I'll fold it into this commit before it lands. Otherwise, if the coinage is fine, one option is a one-time definition in `ring-buffer-design.md` or `ARCHITECTURE.md` at close-out (which already has a doc-sync step) so future readers have an anchor for the term.

> DEVELOPER

I suggest using some like "The crate exposes multiple versions of the crate so that historical versions are available for testing and perforamnce comparision. This is done using the Rust re-export technique." Thoughts

> DEVELOPER

I suggest using the re-export technique and something like "The crate exposes multiple versions of the crate so that historical versions are available for testing and perforamnce comparision. This is done using the Rust re-export technique." Thoughts

> AGENT

Agree with the direction — plain "re-export" is the searchable, accurate term, and the mechanism genuinely is nothing more than that. Two refinements to your draft sentence:

- **"multiple versions of the crate" → "multiple versions of each primitive"** — the crate is one version; it's the primitive modules (`spsc`, `mpsc`, `pool`) that each carry versioned `vN` siblings. Worth keeping that precise, since per-primitive independent versioning was an explicit decision in the ladder.
- **Keep the "default" idea explicit** — the one thing the old coinage was doing is naming which version the unversioned names (`spsc::Ring`) point at. The doc should still tell a future editor "to change the default, repoint this re-export" — that's the actionable part.

Concrete proposal, e.g. for `mpsc/mod.rs`:

```rust
//! MPSC ring: versioned sibling implementations.
//!
//! - Each `vN` submodule is a complete, live implementation;
//!   historical versions stay available for testing and
//!   performance comparison, pinned by explicit path
//!   (`mpsc::v0::MpscRing`).
//! - The re-export below selects the crate's default version;
//!   repoint it at another `vN` to change the default without
//!   touching type names or call sites.
```

Same shape for `spsc/mod.rs`. There are two more "champion" sites to sweep for consistency: the `lib.rs` crate docstring ("behind per-module champion aliases; this crate root re-exports the champions" → "behind per-module default-version re-exports") and the todo.md decisions bullet ("a champion alias per module" → "a default-version re-export per module"), which matters because that block moves into chores verbatim at close-out.

All four edits are small and belong in this commit — the `spsc/mod.rs` edit touches a file the previous push landed, but editing it now is just a normal follow-on change, no history rewrite. Want me to apply them and re-show the (slightly expanded) commit description?

```
vc-x1 0.67.1

Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.

Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a
single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a
state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can
resume without re-doing completed stages.

Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)
→ message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →
commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both
→ push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /
commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via
`jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.
After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
recovery is forward-only.

Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes
to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body
across resumes so only the first invocation needs them.

Usage: vc-x1 push [OPTIONS] [BOOKMARK]

Arguments:
  [BOOKMARK]
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (positional form of `--bookmark`).
          
          Accepting a positional lets the common case read as `vc-x1 push main`
          without the `--bookmark` ceremony; `--bookmark` is kept as an alias
          for scripts and for composition with other args. The two forms
          conflict if both supplied.

Options:
      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (flag form; see positional)

  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
          
          Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after printing): the
          banner now rides along with normal subcommand execution rather than
          gating it, so scripts can capture the version *and* the command's
          output in one invocation.

      --restart
          Clear any saved state file and start from stage 1

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --from <STAGE>
          Explicit stage to jump to (advanced / debug use)

          Possible values:
          - preflight:       Run fmt / clippy / test / install / retest
          - review:          Present diff for the first approval gate
          - message:         Compose / edit the commit message; present for
            second gate
          - commit-app:      Commit the app repo
          - commit-claude:   Commit the `.claude` session repo (skipped if
            empty)
          - bookmark-both:   Advance both bookmarks to `@-`
          - push-app:        `jj git push --bookmark <b> -R .`
          - finalize-claude: `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <b>
            ...`

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

      --step
          Pause between every stage for an interactive approval gate

      --status
          Print where the saved state thinks we are and exit

      --recheck
          Re-run `preflight` even on resume (default: skip if last run
          succeeded)

      --no-finalize
          Stop before `finalize-claude` so it can be run manually

      --dry-run
          Print the exact commands for every stage without side effects

      --title <STR>
          Commit title (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

      --body <STR>
          Commit body (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

  -y, --yes
          Auto-approve interactive prompts (review gate, message-edit
          confirmation). Required when `--title` / `--body` aren't both supplied
          in non-interactive contexts

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
```

```
//! Message pool: fixed-size buffers over a caller-provided
//! region, handed out through an intrusive LIFO free-stack —
//! the allocation half of the messaging layer, decoupling
//! "get a message" from "send it" (see the design doc's
//! "Messaging layer: pools and descriptor queues").
//!
//! - One `#[repr(C)]` [`PoolHeader`] spanning two cache lines
//!   (immutable geometry, free-stack head), followed by
//!   `buf_count` buffers of `buf_size` bytes each.
//! - The free-stack is intrusive with zero per-buffer
//!   overhead: a *free* buffer's first word holds the index
//!   of the next free buffer; an allocated buffer carries no
//!   header at all (in-buffer provenance is deferred — the
//!   descriptor is the travel form; see the design doc's
//!   "Descriptor and registry design (0.7.0)").
//! - One owning allocator pops ([`Pool::alloc`], `&mut self`
//!   as the in-process single-popper token); any holder
//!   frees ([`BufSlot::free`], the MPSC push side). The
//!   [`BufSlot`] guard does not borrow the pool, so any
//!   number of allocated buffers may be live at once —
//!   the decoupling the messaging layer exists for.

use core::marker::PhantomData;
use core::mem::size_of;
use core::ops::{Deref, DerefMut};
use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
use zerocopy::{FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};

use crate::{CACHE_LINE_SIZE, CacheAligned, Error, check_type};
```

Work complete for `0.12.0-3` refactor: pool into a v0 module dir. The simplest of the three moves:

- `src/pool.rs` → `src/pool/v0/mod.rs` — moved unchanged (jj detects it as a pure rename); it only imports via `crate::`, so no edits needed.
- `src/pool/mod.rs` — new outer module, same shape as the other two: versioned-siblings docstring (already in the plain re-export wording), `pub mod v0`, default-version re-exports of `BufSlot`, `Exhausted`, `Pool`, `PoolHeader`, `PoolResolver`.
- `src/lib.rs` — `mod pool` → `pub mod pool`, so `pool::v0::Pool` is reachable for pinning; crate-root re-exports unchanged. `registry.rs`'s `use crate::pool::PoolResolver` resolves through the new re-export untouched.
- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.12.0-2` → `0.12.0-3`.
- `notes/todo.md` — ladder marker `-3` done.

Clippy `-D warnings` clean, all 40 tests pass. Ready to commit. Please review.

> DEVELOPER

What about registry and policy, registry could fit into pool

> AGENT

Both were consciously left out of this cycle — here's the reasoning, and my take on the pool suggestion:

**Policy** is shared-core, not a primitive. `policy.rs` holds wait-policy helpers (`policy::spin`) consumed by both ring flavors' endpoints — the same category as `Error`, `Full`/`Empty`, `slot_ptr` in `lib.rs`. There's no protocol in it to fork into a `v1`, so it has no version axis; versioning it would just add a directory level with nothing to select between.

**Registry** — the In Progress decisions block explicitly deferred it: "registry stays top-level — its placement waits for the descriptor-queue endpoints work." On "registry could fit into pool": it's genuinely pool-adjacent (it maps `PoolId` → pool regions, and its one dependency is `pool::PoolResolver`), so nesting it under `pool/` isn't wrong today. But I'd stick with the deferral, for two reasons:

- The consumer that will actually shape it is Todo #1, the descriptor-queue endpoints (`DescSender`/`DescReceiver`), which own "ring endpoint + registry access" and are designed against both ring flavors. That work may want registry as a peer of the messaging layer (rings + pools + descriptors) rather than inside one primitive — deciding now pre-empts a design that's one cycle away.
- If registry did move under `pool/`, the follow-on question is whether it goes in `pool/v0/` (versioned with the pool's layout) or `pool/` (unversioned) — and that's exactly the coupling question the descriptor work will answer, since `Desc` resolution spans pool layout and queue protocol.

So my recommendation: land `-3` as-is, keep both top-level, and let the descriptor-queue cycle place registry with full information. If you'd rather fold registry under `pool/` now, it's a small mechanical addition to this commit — your call.

1	# Ring buffer design
2	
3	Design notes for the zero-copy ring buffer. This file uses
4	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It covers terminology,
5	requirements, constraints, the memory layout, the API, and
6	the validation ladder, and is kept in sync with the
7	implementation in `src/` (as-built): `lib.rs` holds the
8	region-level machinery (header, init/attach/split, geometry
9	helpers) and re-exports the endpoint modules `producer.rs`
10	(Producer, WriteSlot, Full) and `consumer.rs` (Consumer,
11	ReadSlot, Empty).
12	
13	## Goal
14	
15	A ring buffer for passing typed messages between a producer and
16	a consumer with no copying at the boundary: the producer writes
17	its message directly into buffer memory, the consumer reads it
18	in place. The same layout must work intra-process (threads) and
19	inter-process (shared memory).
20	
```
