77	1. Descriptor queue endpoints: paired DescSender (loan +
78	   send) / DescReceiver (recv) [[11]]:
79	   - own ring endpoint + registry access;
80	   - the demo's ~20-line send path becomes ~3 lines;
81	   - `resolve`'s unsafe is audited once inside the crate
82	     (recv safe by construction);
83	   - guard handed back on Full;
84	   - design against both ring flavors (SPSC + MPSC);
85	   - the sender is also where each sender's private
86	     overflow pending list will live.
87	2. Overflow FIFO: on ring Full, append the message to a
88	   sender-private pending list instead of failing
89	   [details](ring-buffer-design.md#overflow-fifo-future):
90	   - intrusive — the same embedded next-link the
91	     free-stack uses, so zero allocation;
92	   - naturally bounded by pool capacity;
93	   - composes per-sender with MPSC — see
94	     [Overflow readiness](ring-buffer-design.md#overflow-readiness).
95	3. Explore why zcr-mpsc-2t measures faster than zcr-with-2t
96	   (73.9 vs 100.1 ns adjusted mean at 300s, 1p/1c) and
97	   whether the mechanism can improve SPSC [[20]]:
98	   - we think SPSC bounces two index lines per handoff
99	     (each side polls the line the other writes) while
100	     MPSC's only shared hot word is the slot seq;
101	   - verify with perf cache-transfer counters and/or the
102	     padded-seq variant (a design open question);
103	   - if confirmed, a seam-word variant might feed back
104	     into the SPSC protocol.
105	4. Batch alloc/free demo: alongside the one-message
106	   alloc_free_1t loops, a variant that allocs X messages
107	   (5, 10, …) then frees them all, pool vs global
108	   allocator. We think the pool's rate stays constant
109	   (pop/push is O(1) regardless of live count, LIFO keeps
110	   the working set hot) while Box::new/drop slows as the
111	   batch outgrows malloc's thread-cache fast path — the
112	   demo should show it.
113	5. Endpoint claims word: CAS-claimed producer/consumer roles
114	   in the ring header so a second attach/split claimant gets
115	   an error instead of silently violating SPSC; costs a
116	   layout_version bump (or spends `_pad0`)
117	   [details](ring-buffer-design.md#resolved-questions).
118	6. Typed endpoints: `Producer<T>` / `Consumer<T>` validating
119	   `T`'s geometry once at split instead of asserting on every
120	   reserve_slot_with [details](ring-buffer-design.md#api).
121	
122	## Ideas
123	
124	- Perf benches live in
125	  [iiac-perf](https://github.com/winksaville/iiac-perf)
126	  (sibling repo `../iiac-perf`), not here — its calibrated
127	  harness compares zc-ring against mpsc et al. directly
128	  (`zcring-1t`/`zcring-2t` mirroring `mpsc_1t`/`mpsc_2t`).
129	  An in-repo bench only if per-commit regression tracking
130	  proves necessary.
131	
132	- Fan-in helper: consumer-side composition polling N SPSC
133	  rings under a pluggable service policy (priority,
134	  round-robin, weighted)
135	  [details](ring-buffer-design.md#fan-in-composition-not-a-mode):
136	  - buildable today from shipped parts;
137	  - likely offered alongside the MPSC ring eventually — no
138	    commitment yet.
139	- Study [iceoryx2](https://github.com/eclipse-iceoryx/iceoryx2)
140	  before implementing message pools — battle-tested loan/send
141	  decoupling and pool-offset machinery; how it differs from
142	  this project in
143	  [Prior art: iceoryx2](ring-buffer-design.md#prior-art-iceoryx2).
144	- `#[global_allocator]` experiment over size-class pools:
145	  GlobalAlloc is `&self` + any-thread, so it needs
146	  shared-allocation pools (phase 2 gen-tagged head) or
147	  per-thread pools with a routing layer; arbitrary `Layout`
148	  needs size-class selection + an oversize fallback. Frees
149	  from any thread are already natural (MPSC push). Classic
150	  mempool → malloc arc; measure the object-pool form in
151	  iiac-perf first.
152	- Private per-handle cache in front of the shared free-stack
153	  (tcache-over-arenas): alloc/free hit a thread-private list
154	  with plain load/store; refill/flush moves batches to the
155	  CAS stack, amortizing one CAS over N messages. Motivating
156	  datum: 2 uncontended CAS = 8.7 ns of the pool's 9.9 ns
157	  single-thread round trip (vs malloc tcache's zero
158	  atomics). Hold until iiac-perf shows per-op CAS matters in
159	  a composed workload; we think the pool's tail latency
160	  (p99, stddev) already beats malloc — no arena locks, no
161	  brk/mmap — and that matters more than the mean.
162	- `Message` trait over the payload cast boilerplate: const
163	  `MSG_ID` + the zerocopy bounds; receiver-side dispatch
164	  (read tag, match, cast) without per-call-site ceremony,
165	  and maybe a transport seam so an embedded
166	  pointer-descriptor profile slots in behind the same API
167	  [details](ring-buffer-design.md#descriptor-and-registry-design-070).
168	- BufSlot auto-free on Drop (RAII, iceoryx2-style): kills the
169	  silent leak-on-drop footgun at the cost of guard-type
170	  asymmetry (ring guards' drop = do-nothing) and a
171	  ManuallyDrop dance in free/send paths. Decide when
172	  descriptor-queue send lands — explicit free is easier to
173	  upgrade than to walk back.
174	- Blocking layer above the crate (futex, eventfd, async
175	  wakers) built on the header's user line — mechanism and
176	  contracts in
177	  [Blocking and user words](ring-buffer-design.md#blocking-and-user-words);
178	  possibly a companion wrapper crate so independent peers
179	  share one protocol.
180	- loom-based exhaustive ordering exploration of the SPSC
181	  protocol.
182	- Polish: `Error` implements `Display` + `core::error::Error`;
183	  `occupancy()` / `is_empty()` accessors.
184	- Packed-slot variant (drop the cache-line-multiple slot
185	  constraint) for small-message space efficiency.
186	- Per-target / configurable `CACHE_LINE` (128 for Apple
187	  M-series false sharing, tiny for cache-less MCUs) — safe
188	  since attach validates the header's `cache_line`; decide
189	  values from iiac-perf measurements.
190	- Embedded floor: protocol is atomic load/store only (no
191	  CAS), so thumbv6m works today; keep it that way where
192	  possible (endpoint claims wants CAS — gate it), and 8/16-bit
193	  targets would need index-width genericization.
194	- Shared `Geometry` struct (`slot_size`, `capacity`, `mask`)
195	  held by Ring and passed whole to the endpoint
196	  constructors, slimming their signatures and Ring's fields.
197	- Black-box test split: move the public-API protocol tests
198	  (roundtrip, abandoned guards, threaded stress) to
199	  `tests/protocol.rs`; white-box tests (u32 wrap, attach
200	  header internals) stay in lib.rs. Do it when a trybuild
201	  compile-fail harness lands there too (pins the
202	  "second reservation does not compile" guarantee).
203	
204	## Done
205	
206	Completed tasks are moved from `## Todo` to here, `## Done`, as they are completed

Give\neach primitive a `{primitive}/v0/` module dir so all three\nare siblings and a future `v1` slots in beside `v0` as a\npeer.\n\nDecisions (the versioning discussion, folded in):\n\n- **Structure** — `src/spsc/v0/`, `src/mpsc/v0/`,\n  `src/pool/v0/`; version is the inner axis, so each\n  primitive versions independently.\n- **Names unchanged** — keep the current type names inside\n  `v0` (`Ring`, `MpscRing`, `Pool`, …); a default-version\n  re-export per module (`spsc::Ring = spsc::v0::Ring`) plus\n  the existing crate-root re-exports keep the public API\n  identical, so the cycle is a pure move. `spsc::v0::Ring` is\n  newly reachable for explicit version pinning.\n- **Shared core stays in `lib.rs`** — `Error`,\n  `CACHE_LINE_SIZE`, `USER_WORDS`, `slot_ptr`, `check_type`,\n  `CacheAligned` are crate-wide; versions reach them via\n  `crate::`.\n- **`Full` / `Empty` promote to the shared core** — defined\n  in the SPSC endpoint files today but imported by mpsc\n  (`crate::{Empty, Full}`); leaving them inside `spsc/v0/`\n  would couple sibling primitives (and any future\n  `spsc::v1`) to one version's module. Crate-root re-exports\n  keep the public API unchanged.\n- **`-1` also moves the SPSC core out of `lib.rs`** —\n  `Header`, `Ring`, `MAGIC`, `LAYOUT_VERSION`, `header_ptr`,\n  `region_size`, `validate_geometry` go into `spsc/v0/`\n  alongside `producer.rs` / `consumer.rs`.\n- **registry stays top-level** — its placement waits for the\n  descriptor-queue endpoints work.\n- **Cargo feature-gating deferred** — coexistence needs no\n  features; per-version build-size exclusion only pays off\n  once a `v1` exists, so features land with the first fork.\n- **Close-out doc sync** — `ring-buffer-design.md`'s\n  as-built intro names top-level `producer.rs` /\n  `consumer.rs`; sync it to the new layout at close-out.\n\nPlan ladder:\n\n- `0.12.0-0` chore: open versioned module dir refactor\n  (done)\n- `0.12.0-1` refactor: spsc into a v0 module dir (done)\n- `0.12.0-2` refactor: mpsc into a v0 module dir (done)\n- `0.12.0-3` refactor: pool into a v0 module dir (done)\n- `0.12.0` refactor: versioned primitive module dirs",

Validated against GitHub, and the current reference is actually correct there — the proposed change would break it:

- The heading `### Follow-on: endpoints and wait policies` renders on github.com with anchor id `user-content-follow-on-endpoints-and-wait-policies` (fetched just now from the live page). That matches the existing `[11]: chores/chores-01.md#follow-on-endpoints-and-wait-policies`, and also the in-file link at chores-01.md:458 which uses the same slug.
- This agrees with the algorithm recorded in AGENTS.md: the colon is *stripped*, not converted — only its trailing space becomes a hyphen, so no doubling.
- A detail worth noting: colon-to-hyphen conversion would produce `follow-on--endpoints-...` (double hyphen *after* "on", where the colon sits), but your working link had `follow--on-...` (double hyphen between "follow" and "on") — so whatever renderer accepted that is doing something else again, possibly treating the existing hyphen in "Follow-on" specially.

So we have a renderer discrepancy, not a broken ref: GitHub wants the current single-hyphen form. Where did the single-hyphen link fail for you — Zed's markdown preview, VS Code, or somewhere else? If it's the editor preview, the refs should stay GitHub-canonical (that's what the notes conventions target) and we'd just note the editor quirk; changing the ref would fix the preview but break the published page.

> DEVELOPER

You say "Zed's markdown preview generates different anchors for headings"
that isn't correct we generate the anchors.

Actually, there is a `[n]` in the body and then of a corresponding left
justified "thing" of the form `[n]: urlish`, please help with the

> AGENT

Right — good catch on who does what. Two things happen: **we** write the fragment (`#follow-on-...`) into the notes, and the **renderer** auto-generates an id for each heading and then matches our fragment against it. GitHub and VS Code generate github-slugger-style ids so our fragment resolves; Zed's preview evidently generates (or matches) heading ids differently, so the same fragment finds nothing there. The caveat should say that.

On terminology, CommonMark has exact names for both pieces:

- `[n]` in the body is a **reference link** — specifically a *shortcut reference link* (just the label; `[text][n]` with separate text is a *full reference link*). Our doubled `[[n]]` form is what AGENTS.md calls a *citation* — a literal `[` + the reference link + `]`.
- The left-justified `[n]: urlish "title"` line is a **link reference definition**. Its parts: `[n]` is the *link label*, the urlish part is the *link destination*, and the optional quoted string is the *link title* (where we stash the 40-hex SHA in commit refs).
- Within the destination, the `#...` part is the **fragment** (fragment identifier), and the auto-generated heading id it must match is the **heading anchor** (the slug).

Here's the corrected caveat text:

> Renderer caveat: our link reference definitions write GitHub-style fragments (`#follow-on-endpoints-and-wait-policies`); each renderer auto-generates its own heading anchors and matches the fragment against them. As of July 2026 that resolves on GitHub and in VS Code (both verified) but 404s in Zed's markdown preview, whose generated anchors differ. A fragment that fails only in Zed's preview is not broken; GitHub is the reference.

Want me to apply that wording (and I can also fix "citation"/"definition" nearby if anything reads loosely)?

No existing issue matches — here's what I found in the Zed repo:

**Issue search:** Nothing on anchor/slug mismatches. The closest are #18699 (the feature request for clickable heading anchors, closed by the implementation) and #58270 (preview links doing nothing at all on Windows — a different failure).

**But the source complicates the story.** The anchor feature landed 2026-04-09 via PR #53184, and the slugger currently on `main` (`generate_heading_slug` in `crates/util/src/markdown.rs`) keeps hyphens, *drops* colons, and maps spaces to hyphens — which produces exactly the GitHub slug `follow-on-endpoints-and-wait-policies` for our heading. Its unit tests even pin `"Hello-World"` → `"hello-world"`. So by the current code, the canonical fragment should resolve in Zed.

That leaves two candidate explanations for what you saw:

- **Your installed Zed predates or diverges from that code** — worth checking your version against April 2026.
- **The bug is in fragment *matching*, not slug generation** — our `[11]` is a cross-file reference (`chores/chores-01.md#...` — note: relative subdir path without a `./` prefix, where PR #53184's stated test case was `./other.md#heading`). The generation side being correct doesn't rule out the lookup side mangling the comparison.

One detail that doesn't fit any theory yet: a colon-to-hyphen conversion would yield `follow-on--endpoints-...` (double hyphen where the colon was), but the variant you reported working was `follow--on-...` (double hyphen inside "Follow-on"). If that wasn't a typo, Zed is doing something to the pre-existing hyphen itself, which no version of their slugger I can see explains.

To file a solid issue I'd want: your Zed version (`zed --version` or the About dialog), and whether the failure reproduces with a *same-file* link (`[x](#follow-on-endpoints-and-wait-policies)` inside chores-01.md itself) or only cross-file. use std::fmt::{Display, Formatter};

/// Generates a URL-friendly slug from heading text (e.g. "Hello World" → "hello-world").
pub fn generate_heading_slug(text: &str) -> String {
    text.trim()
        .chars()
        .filter_map(|c| {
            if c.is_alphanumeric() || c == '-' || c == '_' {
                Some(c.to_lowercase().next().unwrap_or(c))
            } else if c == ' ' {
                Some('-')
            } else {
                None
            }
        })
        .collect()
}

> AGENT

That code settles the search-side theory, actually — and points the finger back at generation. Here's the chain:

- The click handler (`markdown_preview_view.rs:867`) takes the fragment verbatim and `scroll_to_heading` looks it up in the `heading_slugs` HashMap — an exact string match, no colon handling anywhere. The search can't "mishandle" a colon; it either finds the key or doesn't.
- So if adding the extra dash makes it scroll, the HashMap key *is* the double-dash string — Zed's table was built with that slug. The bug is in what `build_heading_slugs` + `generate_heading_slug` produced, not in the matching.
- Which is genuinely weird, because the accumulation you see above concatenates raw source slices (`heading_text.push_str(&source[range])`), so `heading_text` should be exactly `Follow-on: endpoints and wait policies`, and the v1.9.0 slugger provably drops the colon and yields the canonical single-dash slug. Reading the code, this bug shouldn't exist — yet it does on your machine. One suspect visible here: the `SubstitutedText` arm (parser.rs:152) pushes *substituted* text instead of source text — if some substitution fires inside your heading, the slugger sees altered input.

There's one measurement that would pin it down, and only you can take it — the exact position of the working extra dash. Two candidates, and they implicate different code:

1. `#follow-on--endpoints-and-wait-policies` (double dash where the colon was) → the colon is being *converted* to a dash rather than dropped — slugger input or a stale/divergent slugger.
2. `#follow--on-endpoints-and-wait-policies` (double dash inside "Follow-on") → something is mangling the pre-existing hyphen — much stranger, likely that `SubstitutedText` path.

Earlier you typed variant 2, but a typo would be easy. Could you check which of the two actually scrolls in Zed 1.9? With that answer I can draft a precise upstream issue — minimal repro (`### Follow-on: endpoints and wait policies` + both fragment variants), your version, and the code-path analysis above.

fn handle_url_click(
    url: SharedString,
    view: &WeakEntity<MarkdownPreviewView>,
    base_directory: Option<PathBuf>,
    workspace: &WeakEntity<Workspace>,
    window: &mut Window,
    cx: &mut App,
) {
    let (path_part, fragment) = split_local_url_fragment(url.as_ref());

    if path_part.is_empty() {
        if let Some(fragment) = fragment {
            let view = view.clone();
            let slug = SharedString::from(fragment.to_string());
            window.defer(cx, move |window, cx| {
                if let Some(view) = view.upgrade() {
                    let markdown = view.read(cx).markdown.clone();
                    let active_editor = view
                        .read(cx)
                        .active_editor
                        .as_ref()
                        .map(|state| state.editor.clone());

                    let source_index =
                        markdown.update(cx, |markdown, cx| markdown.scroll_to_heading(&slug, cx));

                    if let Some(source_index) = source_index {
                        if let Some(editor) = active_editor {
                            MarkdownPreviewView::move_cursor_to_source_index(
                                &editor,
                                source_index,
                                window,
                                cx,
                            );
                        }
                    }
                }
            });
        }
    } else {
        open_preview_url(
            SharedString::from(path_part.to_string()),
            base_directory,
            workspace,
            window,
            cx,
        );
    }
}

fn open_preview_url(
    url: SharedString,
    base_directory: Option<PathBuf>,
    workspace: &WeakEntity<Workspace>,
    window: &mut Window,
    cx: &mut App,
) {
    let (path_text, _) = split_preview_url(url.as_ref());

    // URL-decode the path for proper handling of encoded characters
    let decoded_path = urlencoding::decode(path_text).unwrap_or_else(|_| Cow::Borrowed(path_text));

    if let Some(workspace) = workspace.upgrade() {
        workspace.update(cx, |workspace, cx| {
            workspace.open_url_or_file(&decoded_path, base_directory.as_deref(), window, cx);
        });
    } else {
        cx.open_url(url.as_ref());
    }
}

fn split_preview_url(url: &str) -> (&str, Option<&str>) {
    match url.split_once('#') {
        Some((path, fragment)) => (path, Some(fragment)),
        None => (url, None),
    }
}

fn resolve_preview_image(
    dest_url: &str,
    base_directory: Option<&Path>,
    workspace_directory: Option<&Path>,
) -> Option<ImageSource> {
    if dest_url.starts_with("data:") {
        return None;
    }

    if dest_url.starts_with("http://") || dest_url.starts_with("https://") {
        return Some(ImageSource::Resource(Resource::Uri(SharedUri::from(
            dest_url.to_string(),
        ))));
    }

    let decoded = urlencoding::decode(dest_url)
        .map(|decoded| decoded.into_owned())
        .unwrap_or_else(|_| dest_url.to_string());

    if let Some(stripped) = ['/', '\\']
        .iter()
        .find_map(|prefix| decoded.strip_prefix(*prefix))
    {
        if let Some(root) = workspace_directory {
            let absolute_path = root.join(stripped);
            if absolute_path.exists() {
                return Some(ImageSource::Resource(Resource::Path(Arc::from(
                    absolute_path.as_path(),
                ))));

> AGENT

Empirical confirmation on 1.10 — even better for the report. Here's the draft issue:

---

**Title:** Markdown preview: cross-file anchor links open the file but discard the `#fragment`

**Body:**

### Summary

Clicking a cross-file anchor link (`[text](other.md#some-heading)`) in the Markdown preview opens the target file, but never scrolls to the heading — the fragment is silently dropped. Same-file anchor links (`[text](#some-heading)`) work correctly.

PR #53184 (which added anchor-link support, and closed #18699) states in its description that cross-file anchor links should "open the file, scroll the preview, and move the editor cursor to the heading" — that last part doesn't happen.

### Reproduction steps

1. Create `a.md`:
   ```markdown
   [go to section](b.md#section-two)
   ```
2. Create `b.md`:
   ```markdown
   # Section one

   (enough lines to require scrolling)

   # Section two
   ```
3. Open `a.md`'s Markdown preview and click the link.

### Current vs. expected behavior

- Current: `b.md` opens, positioned at the top; the `#section-two` fragment is ignored.
- Expected: `b.md` opens scrolled to "Section two" (per PR #53184's description).

### Cause

In `crates/markdown_preview/src/markdown_preview_view.rs`, `handle_url_click` routes any link with a non-empty path part to `open_preview_url`, whose first line splits the fragment off and discards it:

```rust
let (path_text, _) = split_preview_url(url.as_ref());
```

No `scroll_to_heading` is attempted after the file opens. The same-file branch (empty path part) does call `scroll_to_heading`, which is why same-file anchors work. Verified present in v1.9.0 ([L1008 equivalent](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/v1.9.0/crates/markdown_preview/src/markdown_preview_view.rs#L903)) and v1.10.0 (L1008); behavior confirmed on both.

Nice bit of archaeology — that likely explains why nobody *noticed* (they don't dogfood numbered link reference definitions in their own docs), but I don't think it changes the mechanism. Two reasons:

- Your reference links *do* work in Zed except for the fragment — the file opens. So Zed parses `[11]` → `[11]: chores/chores-01.md#...` fine (pulldown-cmark resolves reference definitions before Zed ever sees the link; by the time `handle_url_click` runs, a reference link and an inline link are the same resolved URL string).
- The discard in `open_preview_url` happens on that resolved URL, so it should hit **inline** cross-file anchors (`[text](b.md#section-two)`) just as hard. Link style shouldn't matter.

That said, my drafted repro uses an inline link and I've only actually *observed* the failure through your reference links — worth 30 seconds to close the gap. If you paste `[go](chores/chores-01.md#follow-on-endpoints-and-wait-policies)` inline into notes/todo.md and click it in the preview:

- **Scrolls correctly** → link style *does* matter somehow, my code reading missed something, and the issue should be reframed around reference-style links specifically.

---

**Title:** Markdown preview: cross-file anchor links open the file but discard the `#fragment`

**Body:**

### Summary

Clicking a cross-file anchor link (`[text](other.md#some-heading)`) in the Markdown preview opens the target file but never scrolls to the heading — the fragment is silently dropped. Same-file anchor links (`#some-heading`) work. Both inline links and reference-style links (`[label]` + `[label]: other.md#heading` definitions) are affected, as expected since they resolve to the same URL before click handling.

PR #53184 (which added anchor-link support, closing #18699) states cross-file anchor links should "open the file, scroll the preview, and move the editor cursor to the heading" — the scroll/cursor part doesn't happen.

### Reproduction steps

1. Create `a.md` containing: `[go to section](b.md#section-two)`
2. Create `b.md` with `# Section one`, enough lines to require scrolling, then `# Section two`
3. Open `a.md`'s Markdown preview and click the link.

### Current vs. expected behavior

- Current: `b.md` opens positioned at the top; `#section-two` is ignored.
- Expected: `b.md` opens scrolled to "Section two", editor cursor moved there (per PR #53184).

### Cause

In `crates/markdown_preview/src/markdown_preview_view.rs`, `handle_url_click` routes any link with a non-empty path part to `open_preview_url`, whose first line discards the fragment:

```rust
let (path_text, _) = split_preview_url(url.as_ref());
```

No `scroll_to_heading` is attempted after the file opens. The same-file branch (empty path part) does call `scroll_to_heading`, which is why same-file anchors work. Present in [v1.9.0](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/v1.9.0/crates/markdown_preview/src/markdown_preview_view.rs#L903) and [v1.10.0](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/v1.10.0/crates/markdown_preview/src/markdown_preview_view.rs#L1008); behavior confirmed on both.

(Possibly why it went unnoticed: the zed repo's own markdown barely uses cross-file anchors — `rg -P '\[\d+\]: '` finds no reference-definition usage outside code comments.)

### Zed version and system specs

Zed: v1.10.0 (also reproduced on v1.9.0)
OS: Linux (Arch, kernel 7.1.3-arch1-1)

Your regex wasn't wrong — it just answered a narrower question than the one that matters for the bug. `\[\d+\]: ` finds only *numeric* reference definitions (`[1]: url`, the style this project's notes use). Two things it can't see:

- Reference definitions with word labels — `[some-label]: url` — which are more common in the wild.
- Inline cross-file anchors — `[text](file.md#heading)` — which don't involve a definition at all, and are the primary form the bug affects.

> AGENT

The broader sweep refines your conclusion rather than overturning it:

- **Reference definitions** (any label, not just numeric): essentially unused — 15 hits total, and they're in an eval fixture and one README. Your regex's verdict stands; widening the label pattern didn't change it.
- **Inline cross-file anchors** (`file.md#heading`): heavily used — dozens of files under `docs/src/` with 4–22 each. So Zed's repo is *full* of exactly the link form the bug breaks.

But those live in `docs/`, which is their mdBook-published website (zed.dev/docs) — contributors read it rendered on the web, not through Zed's own markdown preview. So the dogfooding-gap explanation survives in a sharper form: it's not that they never *write* cross-file anchors, it's that they never *consume* them through the preview feature that's broken. Worth phrasing the issue's footnote that way instead of the reference-definition observation — it's both more accurate and more persuasive ("your own docs/ tree is a ready-made repro corpus").

Right — that's consistent with everything we've found. Plain cross-file links (`./docs/src/development/windows.md`, no `#fragment`) go through the same `open_preview_url` path and work, because opening the file is the part that functions. The bug is confined to exactly one thing: when a `#fragment` is present on a cross-file link, it's split off and thrown away, so you land at the top of the file instead of the section. External `https://` links take a different path entirely and are unaffected.

So the full behavior matrix in Zed 1.9/1.10 preview: same-file `#anchor` ✓, cross-file plain ✓, external ✓, cross-file `file.md#anchor` ✗ (opens, doesn't scroll) — a nicely narrow bug, which makes the one-line cause (`let (path_text, _) = ...`) very credible to a maintainer.

Separate loose end in our repo: the AGENTS.md renderer-caveat edits (the Zed fragment-discard explanation) are still uncommitted in the working copy. > DEVELOPER

I see that is just has the path_text, but wouldn't there be a second step which "searched" for the "#" fragment ?

> AGENT

Exactly — and that missing second step *is* the bug. The pieces for it already exist; they're just never wired together on the cross-file path:

- **The lookup machinery exists.** When Zed parses a markdown file (with `parse_heading_slugs: true`), `build_heading_slugs` builds a map of slug → source offset. `scroll_to_heading(&slug)` consults it and returns the heading's source index, and `move_cursor_to_source_index` jumps the editor there. The same-file branch of `handle_url_click` does exactly this sequence — that's your working `#anchor`-within-a-file case.
- **The cross-file branch stops after step one.** It calls `workspace.open_url_or_file(path)` and returns. To honor the fragment it would need to: keep the fragment instead of discarding it, wait for the open to complete (it's asynchronous — the target buffer and its preview don't exist yet at click time), wait for the target's markdown to be parsed so the slug map exists, then run the same `scroll_to_heading` + cursor-move sequence against the *newly opened* file's preview/editor.

So there's no hidden "search for `#` in the target file" step that's malfunctioning — the search infrastructure is all there, but the handoff (carry the fragment across the async file-open, then invoke the lookup on the other side) was never written. That's also why the fix is more than deleting the `_`: the one-liner is where the information is *lost*, but the actual repair is plumbing the fragment through `open_preview_url` → open-completion → `scroll_to_heading`. Worth keeping the issue's framing as-is ("fragment is discarded") since that's the observable defect, and letting the maintainers choose the plumbing.

One nuance for their implementer: the slug map lives in the parsed-markdown entity, so if the target file opens as a plain editor without a preview, the cursor jump would need the file parsed anyway (or a fallback like scanning for the heading text). All the more reason the issue should describe behavior, not prescribe the patch.

> DEVELOPER

Guessitmate the effor to fix it?

> AGENT

A guesstimate (this is inference from reading the code, not measurement):

**For a Zed maintainer who knows this crate: roughly half a day to a day.** Call it a 50–150 line PR. The reasoning:

- The hard parts already exist — slug generation, the slug→offset map, `scroll_to_heading`, `move_cursor_to_source_index`. Nothing new has to be invented.
- The genuinely new work is the async handoff: change `open_preview_url` to accept the fragment, get the opened item back from `workspace.open_url_or_file` (or its underlying open task), wait for the buffer/preview to be ready and parsed, then run the existing scroll+cursor sequence. In GPUI that's a spawn-and-await with entity handles — routine for someone fluent in it, ~30–60 lines.
- Plus a test and one UX decision that's more product than code: if the target file opens as a plain editor with no preview pane, do you just move the cursor to the heading (simplest, and the slug map can be built by parsing the buffer once), or force a preview open? The simple answer keeps it a small PR.

**For an outside contributor new to GPUI: more like 2–4 days**, most of it learning Zed's entity/async model and how workspace item opening reports completion — the domain logic itself stays small.

The risk that could inflate either estimate: if `open_url_or_file` doesn't expose a clean "here's the item once it's open" handle at this call site and the plumbing has to be restructured, the half-day version becomes a full day or two. That's the kind of thing that's only visible from inside the build.

So: small, well-bounded fix, very PR-able — which your issue makes easy to pick up, since it names the exact line where the information is lost and the exact functions that already do the second half.

> AGENT

Done — the draft now says "fragment" wherever it's the dropped piece, and "link" only for the part that works. The one place "anchor link" survives is the PR #53184 sentence, deliberately, since that's the PR's own feature name being quoted.

Still pending from earlier: want the one-line cross-reference to #58270 added ("related but distinct — there all links do nothing on Windows; here links work and only the cross-file fragment is dropped")? After that it's ready to file.

> DEVELOPER

Why call it a "cross-file link", it's an "anchor link"(?) but in issue #58270 j055 called it an "Internal project link" that and in our case we notice that "fragments" are ignored.

> AGENT

Because there's no single canonical name — each term slices it differently, and I picked "cross-file" ad hoc just to contrast with the same-file case. Here's how the candidates map to the actual anatomy of `other.md#some-heading`:

- **RFC 3986** (the URI standard): the whole thing is a *relative reference*, made of a *path* (`other.md`) and a *fragment* (`#some-heading`). "Fragment" is the standards-correct name for the failing part — and, decisively, it's what Zed's own code calls it (`split_local_url_fragment`, the `fragment` variable in `handle_url_click`).
- **"Anchor link"** is web colloquial and ambiguous: most people mean a *same-page* `#target` link; PR #53184 stretched it to cover both cases. Using it for the cross-file case invites exactly the confusion you flagged earlier — the "anchor link" mostly works; only its fragment is dropped.
- **"Internal project link"** (j055's term in #58270) names the *path* half — a relative link to a file in the project — and says nothing about fragments, which is fine for that issue since there *all* clicking is dead.

So the most precise, maintainer-greppable phrasing combines the two standard pieces: **"a relative link to another file that carries a fragment"**, with "fragment" as the star since that's what gets ignored. That would make the title something like:

> Markdown preview: fragment ignored on relative links to other files (`other.md#heading` opens the file but doesn't scroll)

That drops my "cross-file" coinage, aligns with RFC 3986 and Zed's own identifiers, and stays clearly distinct from #58270's terminology. Want me to sweep the draft to that phrasing (title + the few "cross-file" mentions in the body)?

> DEVELOPER

But it actually the "easy" cases are to the same file so using "cross-file" is narrowing the scope. But also for "external" links the preview has no work, I see it pop up a browser instance.

> AGENT

Right on both counts — and that completes a clean three-case taxonomy, each with a different "who does the fragment work":

- **Same-file** (`#heading`): Zed does the work, and does it correctly — the easy case, since the parsed document and its slug map are already in hand.
- **Relative link to another file** (`other.md#heading`): Zed must do the work — open, wait for parse, then scroll — and this is precisely where it drops the fragment instead. The narrowing is correct: this *is* the bug's entire scope.
- **External** (`https://…#heading`): Zed has no work to do at all — `open_preview_url`'s fallback hands the *full* URL, fragment included, to `cx.open_url`, and the browser does the fragment navigation. That's the instance you see popping up.

There's a tidy irony in that: the external path preserves the fragment (by passing the whole URL through untouched), while the internal path is the only one that dismembers the URL and loses a piece. Zed keeps the fragment in the two cases where someone else handles it or it's easy, and drops it in the one case where it actually has work to do.

Given your point, I'd leave the draft's current phrasing alone — the title already leads with "fragment is ignored" and uses "cross-file link" as the scope-narrower, which you've just argued is the right narrowing. The summary already covers all three cases in one sentence.

So the draft is settled except for one open question from earlier: add the one-line "related but distinct: #58270" cross-reference? Then it's ready to file — by you, or by me via `gh` on your word.

> AGENT

That's a real third data point, and it strengthens the issue: PR #53184's own description says cross-file links should "open the file, **scroll the preview**, and move the editor cursor" — which implies a preview of the target should be what appears. Instead Zed opens the raw editor buffer, so even the non-fragment part of the claimed behavior only half-landed. VS Code's choice (a preview link opens the target *as a preview*) is arguably the principled one: you were in rendered-document land when you clicked, so staying in rendered-document land preserves context — dropping you into raw markdown is a mode switch you didn't ask for. Your preference matches the PR's stated intent, so it's not even a matter of opinion the maintainers need to adjudicate — it's in their own spec.

It also matters for the fix's shape: if the fix only moves the *editor* cursor to the heading (the minimal repair), the raw-buffer behavior persists; if the target opens as a preview, the existing `scroll_to_heading` machinery applies directly. Worth one sentence in the issue so the implementer sees both gaps at once — something like: "Additionally, the target currently opens as a raw editor buffer rather than a preview, though the PR describes scrolling *the preview*; VS Code opens the target as a preview, which preserves the reading context."

1	Title: Markdown preview: fragment is ignored when a cross-file link has one (other.md#heading opens the file but doesn't scroll)
2	
3	### Summary
4	
5	Clicking a cross-file link that carries a fragment
6	(`[text](other.md#some-heading)`) in the Markdown preview opens the
7	target file — the link itself works — but the `#some-heading` fragment is
8	silently dropped, so the preview never scrolls to the heading. Everything
9	else works: same-file fragments (`#some-heading`) scroll correctly, plain
10	cross-file links (`other.md`) open correctly, and external URLs are
11	unaffected. Both inline links and reference-style links (`[label]` +
12	`[label]: other.md#heading` definitions) lose the fragment identically,
13	as expected since they resolve to the same URL before click handling.
14	
15	PR #53184 (which added anchor-link support, closing #18699) states
16	cross-file anchor links should "open the file, scroll the preview, and
17	move the editor cursor to the heading" — the scroll/cursor part doesn't
18	happen.
19	
20	### Reproduction steps
21	
22	Reproducible with Zed's own docs:
23	
24	1. Open `docs/src/git.md` from the zed repo and open its Markdown preview.
25	2. Click the link at line 204:
26	   `[All Settings](./reference/all-settings.md#git-worktree-directory)`.
27	3. `all-settings.md` opens at the top; the `## Git Worktree Directory`
28	   heading (line 5467) is never scrolled to.
29	
30	Or minimally:
31	
32	1. Create `a.md` containing: `[go to section](b.md#section-two)`
33	2. Create `b.md` with `# Section one`, enough lines to require scrolling,
34	   then `# Section two`
35	3. Open `a.md`'s Markdown preview and click the link.
36	
37	### Current vs. expected behavior
38	
39	- Current: the target file opens positioned at the top; the fragment is
40	  ignored.
41	- Expected: the target file opens scrolled to the heading, editor cursor
42	  moved there (per PR #53184's description).
43	
44	### Cause
45	
46	In `crates/markdown_preview/src/markdown_preview_view.rs`,
47	`handle_url_click` routes any link with a non-empty path part to
48	`open_preview_url`, whose first line splits the fragment off and discards
49	it:
50	
51	```rust
52	let (path_text, _) = split_preview_url(url.as_ref());
53	```
54	
55	No `scroll_to_heading` is attempted after the file opens. The same-file
56	branch (empty path part) does call `scroll_to_heading`, which is why
57	same-file fragments work. Present in
58	[v1.9.0](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/v1.9.0/crates/markdown_preview/src/markdown_preview_view.rs#L903)
59	and
60	[v1.10.0](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/v1.10.0/crates/markdown_preview/src/markdown_preview_view.rs#L1008);
61	behavior confirmed on both.
62	
63	(Possibly why it went unnoticed: the zed repo's `docs/src/` tree is full
64	of cross-file links with fragments, but those are consumed through the
65	mdBook site at zed.dev/docs rather than through the Markdown preview.)
66	
67	### Zed version and system specs
68	
69	Zed: v1.10.0 (also reproduced on v1.9.0)
70	OS: Linux (Arch, kernel 7.1.3-arch1-1)
71

Good addition — a self-demonstrating README that exhibits the bugs when previewed in the directory. Review notes, most important first:

1. **You've spotted a new fact the issue draft doesn't have**: bullet 2 says the raw file opens "at whatever the current cursor location" — not necessarily at the top. That's sharper than the draft's "opens positioned at the top" (true only for a freshly opened file; if the buffer was already open, you stay wherever you were — arguably more disorienting). Worth syncing the issue draft to say "opens at its previous cursor position (top for a freshly opened file); the fragment is ignored."
2. **Missing the contrast case**: the README lists only failures. One bullet showing what *works* — a same-file fragment link like `[jump to line 15 below](#testing-markdown-anchors)`... better, a link to a heading inside README itself — would let a reader verify the working case and the broken cases from one file. (That contrast is what makes the diagnosis credible.)
3. **Typos/grammar**: line 8 "opens the raw the file" → "opens the raw file"; line 10–12 "at whatever the current cursor location, it does not move the cursor" → "at whatever the current cursor location is; it does not move".
4. **Bullet 3's `[1]`** resolves fine (definition on line 50), though the sentence reads a bit bare — "behave the same way: [this link][1] opens the raw test2.md and does not scroll" with a `[this link][1]` full reference form would make the clickable thing visible.
5. Cosmetic, inherited from the test files: the filler says "line 29" twice (no "line 27"), and the "line N" labels are one off from true line numbers from there down (e.g. "line 15" sits on line 16). Harmless, but since the files exist to make *positions* obvious, aligning them helps.
6. The README has no heading between line 14 and the filler, so there's nothing to scroll *to* within it — if you add the works-case bullet from point 2, it needs a `## something` further down to target.

> DEVELOPER

Should we point out the clicking "previous" on the menu renders in the raw file?

> AGENT

Possibly — but let me make sure I understand the observation first, since I can't reproduce it from here. Which "previous" do you mean? My best guess: after a cross-file link click dumps you in the raw target file, you use Zed's back navigation (Go Back, or a back arrow in the tab bar / menu) expecting to return to the preview you came from — and instead you land in the *raw source* of the originating file, the preview pane having been left behind. Is that it, or something else (e.g. a "previous/next" control inside the preview itself)?

How I'd triage it once confirmed:

- **If it's back-navigation losing preview mode after this bug's click flow** — worth one sentence in the issue as a second symptom of the same root UX gap (the click handler leaving rendered-document land): "navigating back afterward also lands in the raw source, not the originating preview." It strengthens the "should open as a preview" expected-behavior argument without expanding scope.
- **If it's independent of the link click** (back navigation *never* restores a preview pane, regardless of how you left it) — that's its own bug/feature-gap about previews and navigation history, and I'd keep it out of this issue to keep the fragment report crisply actionable; file or raise it separately on Discord.

Which behavior are you seeing?

> DEVELOPER

When you click on "Target section" it scrolls to the bottom of the previewed README. But if you click ont the "<-" back arrow, which is to the left of the window tabs, to jumps/opens the raw README.md file displaying where ever it was.

> AGENT

Got it — that's cleaner (and more interesting) than my guess: it's the *same-file* flow, the one that "works." Click the fragment link → preview scrolls correctly → click the back arrow (Go Back) → instead of returning to your previous spot in the preview, Zed activates the raw README.md editor buffer at wherever its cursor happened to be.

There's a plausible mechanism visible in the code we already read: the same-file branch does two things — scrolls the preview *and* calls `move_cursor_to_source_index` on the underlying editor. That editor cursor jump is almost certainly what lands in Zed's navigation history (that's the normal trigger for history entries). The preview scroll itself likely records nothing. So Go Back pops an *editor* location and faithfully takes you there — raw buffer, stale position — because from the history's perspective, the editor is where you "were." The feature half-integrates with navigation history: it writes an entry against the wrong surface and never records the preview position you'd want to return to.

Should we point it out? Yes, but contained — I'd add it as a short "Additional observation" at the end of the issue rather than a headline symptom, something like:

> Additional observation: even in the working same-file case, anchor navigation integrates oddly with history — after clicking a fragment link in the preview, the Go Back arrow activates the raw source buffer (at its previous cursor position) rather than returning to the prior preview position. We suspect the editor-cursor move in the same-file branch is what lands in the navigation history, not the preview scroll.

It reinforces the issue's real theme — the anchor feature treats the preview as a second-class navigation surface (cross-file: opens raw and drops the fragment; same-file: history returns you to raw) — without diluting the crisply actionable fragment bug. And it's a good Discord talking point in exactly that framing.

Want me to append that note to the draft?

> DEVELOPER

It does "scroll" that raw file to where the cursor is, if you had scrolled away from it.

> AGENT

That detail clinches the mechanism: scrolling-to-cursor is exactly what Go Back does when it pops a navigation-history entry — it takes you to a recorded *cursor location*, not a viewport. So the history entry really is the editor cursor position (the one `move_cursor_to_source_index` set, or wherever the cursor already sat), confirming the preview scroll itself records nothing. Appending the note with that refinement:

We\nsuspect the editor-cursor move in the same-file branch\n(`move_cursor_to_source_index`) is what lands in the navigation\nhistory — Go Back then faithfully returns to an editor location, while\nthe preview scroll itself records nothing. Together with the cross-file\nbehavior above, the pattern is that the preview is a second-class\nnavigation surface: links out of it land in raw buffers, and history\nout of it returns to raw buffers.\n\n### Zed version and system specs"
}

# Testing markdown anchors

It looks like markdown anchor links aren't working well, I see
the following issues when viewing a markdown file in the zed
preview mode (zed v1.10.0; the same links work on GitHub and in
VS Code preview).

What works:

- A same-file fragment link: [Target section](#target-section)
  scrolls this preview down to the heading near the bottom. But
  then clicking the back arrow (left of the window tabs) it
  activates the raw README.md, scrolled to its cursor position,
  instead of returning to the previous preview position.

What fails:

- A link with just a file, [test 1](test1.md), opens the raw
  file, not the preview.
- A link with a file and fragment,
  [test 1 line 50](test1.md#test-1-line-50), opens the raw file
  at whatever the current cursor location is; it does not move
  to the "test 1 line 50" heading.
- Reference links with fragments behave the same way:
  [this link][1] opens the raw test2.md and does not scroll to
  the "test 2 line 100" heading.

> Renderer caveat: our link reference definitions write
> GitHub-style fragments
> (`#follow-on-endpoints-and-wait-policies`); each renderer
> auto-generates its own heading anchors and matches the
> fragment against them. As of July 2026 (Zed v1.9.0)
> cross-file fragments (`file.md#heading`) open the file but
> never scroll to the heading in Zed's markdown preview — the
> fragment is discarded on cross-file navigation
> (`open_preview_url` in
> `crates/markdown_preview/src/markdown_preview_view.rs`).
> GitHub and VS Code resolve the same links fine (both
> verified). A fragment that fails only in Zed's preview is
> not broken; GitHub is the reference.
> 
433,434c447,448
< 1. Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
